**What this is.** The register of record, from 2026-09-29, for the user's
decisions (D-nnn) and for the ideas the user chooses to pursue later (I-nnn).
Work state -- built, pushed, run -- lives in the handoffs, not here; entries
point to where.

**Rules.**
- Read this log before implementing or reporting anything a decision
  constrains, and cite by ID ("per D-015").
- When the user makes a call, append the entry in the same turn: a new
  D-nnn at the end of its section, a row in the index, a row in the
  changelog.
- Never edit an entry in place. Only its **Status** line may change (to
  "superseded by D-nnn" or "done <date>"), dated. A change of mind is a new
  entry that supersedes the old one; the two cross-reference.
- An **idea** (I-nnn) is something the user chose to pursue later. A
  proposal of Claude's that the user has not taken up is not an idea; it
  stays in the handoffs ("proposed, not built").
- Tags: `[user]` the source records the call as the user's;
  `[user, delegated]` the user left the choice to Claude, who made it;
  `[recorded]` recorded as decided, the source does not say by whom;
  `[KB]` a project document, read 2026-09-29. ASCII only, LF only.

|---|---|---|---|

## Decisions

### D-001 -- Standalone DSN training stays in `hpc/dsn`
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-19 / `[user]`
- **Binds:** `~/SBI/hpc/dsn` (`run_optimization.py`, `search.py`, `run_refit.pbs`, ...).
- **Decision:** keep standalone DSN training in the SBI repo's `hpc/dsn` mirror.
- **Why:** not recorded in the source.
- **Implies:** the Stage C retrain (C4 step 1) runs `hpc/dsn/hpc/run_refit.pbs` under `sbi_env`.
- **Does not decide:** the retrain's configuration.
- **Sources:** `[KB]` migration handoff S2 ("decision 2026-09-19"), S5 "Decisions taken (all the user's)".
- **Status:** active.

### D-002 -- The cohort manifest is the preprocessing contract
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-19/21, the day of each part not recorded / `[user]`
- **Binds:** Sbi-extractor `extractor/` and `cohort_manifest.py`; both exports; the DSN training loader.
- **Decision:** the extractor emits the manifest; any mismatch raises; the DSN reads its preprocessing from the manifest and everything else from the config; `mfr_threshold` is gating; the manifest carries `manifest_version`.
- **Statement:** on the real arm the gating set (`GATING_REAL`) is the eight `PREPROCESSING_FIELDS` (`fs_raw`, `index_base`, `grid_width`, `n_subsets`, `electrodes_per_subset`, `mfr_threshold`, `w_size`, `gaussian_window`) plus `n_units` and `manifest_version`; the sim arm is D-005. No `--assume-preprocessing` override; legacy archives are not exported.
- **Why:** one recorded contract for both arms instead of an assumed parity (the parity script it replaces, `check_preprocessing_parity.py`, was retired unrun). The source frames `CohortConfig` as the plan and the manifest as the trace `[reasoning, in the source]`.
- **Implies:** built and run in Stage D (`extracted_v2/cohort_manifest.json`, sha256 `c925c5106137fe82`, 2026-09-21); wiring the consumers is Stage C C1-C2; from commit C2c the archive check also refuses archives without a matching `manifest_version`.
- **Does not decide:** the sim arm's `n_e` (D-015); what is recorded rather than applied on the sim arm (D-005).
- **Sources:** `[KB]` migration handoff S5 "Decisions taken", S6 (rules, field groups), S4.2 (the corrected field list); `EXTRACTOR_USAGE.md` v8 row.
- **Status:** active.

### D-003 -- Everything downstream is re-run; the r2 encoder is not carried forward
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-19/21 / `[user]`
- **Binds:** the frozen encoder (`artifacts/frozen_dsn/`); both exports; gate, witness, NPE.
- **Decision:** re-run everything downstream under the new framework; do not carry the frozen r2 encoder forward.
- **Statement:** the DSN is retrained under `sbi_env` on `extracted_v2`; no checkpoint trained under `meacnn_cpu` is treated as comparable to one trained under `sbi_env`.
- **Why:** the DSN suite passed 30/30 under both environments (`meacnn_cpu`: torch 2.13.0+cu130, pml 2.9.0; `sbi_env`: torch 2.10.0, pml 1.6.3). That licenses the switch; it does not certify numerical identity, and no comparison was run.
- **Implies:** Stage C C4's order (retrain, real-arm export, sim-arm export, gate, witness, NPE); the r2 exports (`ANN/SBI_export_r2/`) are not comparable with anything exported after the retrain (`HPC_PATHS.md` 4d).
- **Does not decide:** the retrain's configuration.
- **Sources:** `[KB]` migration handoff S3.1 (closing paragraph), S5; Stage C handoff C4; `HPC_PATHS.md` sec. 3, 4d.
- **Status:** active. Its retrain is deferred by D-031 (2026-10-01): until it runs, an export of the re-extracted simulations can only use the r2 encoder, provisionally (Open calls). [corrected 2026-10-01 14:22: no export of the re-extracted simulations is made before the retrain (D-033), so the "provisionally" clause is moot and the Open call it points to is closed.]

### D-004 -- The inference coordinate is declared in the label-axes contract and asserted at export
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-20 / `[user]`
- **Binds:** `preflight_label_axes.py`, `artifacts/label_axes.json`, `example_export.py::_load_label_axes`, `build_label_spec`, the shard sidecar.
- **Decision:** freeze the coordinate (ln or linear, per theta column) in the label-axes contract.
- **Statement:** `label_axes.json` declares each column's coordinate; each campaign's `manifest.json` still supplies it, through `registry_from_manifest`; at export the manifest-derived coordinate is asserted against the declaration, and a mismatch raises, naming the axes. A frozen coordinate can only check, never impose: stored theta was fixed by the simulator when the data were generated. **Excluded as fixes:** editing `PARAM_BOUNDS` to move an axis off the threshold, and moving rule (1)'s threshold.
- **Why:** rule (1) infers a modelling decision (the inference coordinate) from the prior bounds, and four active axes (`U_max`, `U_0_sr`, `alpha_syn`, `x0`) sit exactly on its threshold (margin +0.000000, measured 2026-09-20, unchanged 2026-09-21), so any bound edit would flip a column's coordinate.
- **Implies:** Stage C item C3 (decided, not built); `label_axes.json` regenerated once C3 exists.
- **Does not decide:** whether the regenerated freeze's campaign set equals the r2 freeze's (Stage C C3: probably, not verified).
- **Sources:** `[KB]` migration handoff S6b, S3.2, S4.2 (the withdrawn "widen the axis" advice); Stage C handoff C3.
- **Status:** active.

### D-005 -- `mfr_threshold` and `n_subsets` are recorded on the sim arm, never applied
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-21 / `[user]`
- **Binds:** `cohort_manifest.py`'s field groups; the sim-arm sidecar.
- **Decision:** `mfr_threshold` is record-only on the sim arm (option (i)); the real rule is applied on the real arm only. `n_subsets` likewise is recorded and never applied.
- **Statement:** `CONFORMED_SIM = (w_size, gaussian_window)`; `ASSERTED_SIM = (electrodes_per_subset,)`; `RECORDED_SIM = (mfr_threshold, n_subsets, fs_raw, index_base, grid_width)`.
- **Why:** `mfr_threshold` is an electrode-level filter with no simulated analogue, and a simulated recording is a single subregion; `n_subsets` and `n_units = 35 x n_subsets` are real-arm multiplicities, to be recorded and never gated on.
- **Implies:** `sim_preprocessing_from_manifest` returns the `RECORDED_SIM` values for the sidecar.
- **Does not decide:** the sim arm's `n_e` (D-015).
- **Sources:** `[KB]` migration handoff S5 (the 09-21 decisions), S6 (the arms table); Stage C handoff C1.
- **Status:** active. [2026-10-08: D-075, a 0.1 Hz floor on the mean rate of each Giulia simulated window, is a selection on the rows of the Giulia sim bank, not the electrode-level filter this entry keeps record-only in the DUP15HD manifest contract; this entry is unchanged.]

### D-006 -- Scheduler behaviour is measured before it is relied on; the aggregation check is the gate
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-21 / `[user]`
- **Binds:** every PBS array followed by a dependent job (the Stage D aggregation; the C8 re-extraction).
- **Decision:** verify the array dependency by a probe before the real launch; do not assume it.
- **Statement (the probe's result, measured 2026-09-21):** davinci's PBS Pro has no `afterokarray` (a TORQUE keyword; `qsub: illegal -W value`). `afterok:<array id>[]` is accepted but gives ordering only: a subjob exiting 1 does not stop the dependent. So the aggregation check (`cohort_manifest.py` for Stage D) is the gate, and success is never inferred from the array finishing.
- **Why:** two schedulers both called "PBS" diverge exactly here; the probe measured it.
- **Implies:** C8's completion gate is a separate check (Stage C C8 step 3); `launch_stage_d.sh` prints the measurement at submission.
- **Sources:** `[KB]` migration handoff S3.3, S4.2, S5; its header ("Decisions settled today").
- **Status:** active. [2026-10-02 `[CLUSTER]`, the Giulia G1 run: the aggregation `1776557` ran, and REFUSED, after two subjobs of the array `1776556[]` had ended without writing their fragment -- the ordering-only behaviour again. `launch_stage_d.sh`'s submission line "agg : ... (held until every array task exits 0)" (written 2026-09-30, `63d2574`) contradicts this Statement; corrected in the next Sbi-extractor push.] [2026-10-02 15:07 `[CLUSTER]`, the second G1 run: the aggregation `1777367` again ran after two failed subjobs of `1777366[]`, and refused. The launcher line is corrected in Sbi-extractor `0a560ca` (pushed 15:04), which that run had not pulled yet.] [2026-10-02 15:31 `[CLUSTER]`, the third G1 run, at `0a560ca`: the launcher printed `agg   : 1777496.pbsserver01   (runs once the array has ended, whatever its tasks' exit codes)`; the aggregation, the gate, found a usable fragment for each of the 16 listed wells and ended with the PASS line and `[job] cohort_manifest_exit=0`.] [2026-10-16 12:29 `[CLUSTER]`, the Giulia simulated bank: the gate `1845589` (afterok on the array `1845588[]`, 69 elements, both submitted at 12:14) finished WITHOUT RUNNING -- no log, no `BANK_RECORD.json`, no exit status in the pipeline's `qstat -x -f` read; 152 shard files of the plan's 155 on disk `[CLUSTER, the user's paste of 12:44]`. A dependent ended unrun is a case the 2026-09-21 probe (an element exiting 1, the dependent ran) did not cover; the cause is under diagnosis (a read-only check of the gate's, the array's and each element's PBS records, the task logs, the markers, given at 12:57). Until it is known the Statement stands as measured -- an element exiting with a code of its own does not stop the dependent -- and is not read as "the dependent always runs". The pipeline no longer relies on it: a gate that never ran is run alone once the array has ended (SBI `sci/pipeline` `f17b6d6`, D-081's Status).] [2026-10-18 `[CLUSTER, the user's read-only check of the 2026-10-16 run, pasted at 11:43]`, the cause: 66 of the 69 elements exited 0; three failed, all on the node `dvnode012` -- elements 45 and 46 exited 135 (`Bus error (core dumped)` after `[Errno 5] Input/output error` reading the simulation tree under `/davinci-1/home`; 45's traceback also reports the DSN tree missing), element 63 never started (Exit_status -2, walltime 00:00:00, its log only `Could not chdir to home directory`): the node had lost the home file system. The array's record reads Exit_status 0 with the comment `Job Array Began ... and failed`; the gate's reads `depend = afterok:1845588[]...`, job_state F, no start time, no Exit_status. Reading `[reasoning]`, not checked in the server's log: the elements that ran and exited with a code (135 here, 1 in the probe) leave the dependent to run; the element PBS failed to start (-2) is what ended the gate unrun. The Statement holds for elements that run; an element that fails to start (a node fault) ends an afterok dependent unrun, whatever the array's Exit_status reads. Recorded in SBI `specs/SPEC.md` (`6448b95`).] [2026-10-18: the pipeline's simulated bank no longer rests on the dependent running: every round ends with D-094's verification job, which judges the bank whatever the gate did.]

### D-007 -- No byte comparison of old vs new archives
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-21 / `[user]`
- **Binds:** Stage D and Stage C validation.
- **Decision:** no `cmp` of the old (`extracted/`) against the new (`extracted_v2/`) archives.
- **Why:** everything is re-extracted.
- **Sources:** `[KB]` migration handoff S5.
- **Status:** active.

### D-008 -- `CohortConfig` lives in the SBI repo; Sbi-extractor reads it
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-21 / `[user]`
- **Binds:** `hpc/dsn/cohort.py` (SBI); `cohort_config.py` (Sbi-extractor).
- **Decision:** `CohortConfig` lives in the SBI repo (`hpc/dsn/cohort.py`, torch-free); Sbi-extractor reads it through `cohort_config.py`; the dependency direction is unchanged, Sbi-extractor -> SBI only.
- **Why:** one torch-free definition, re-exported by `config.py` and `make_mea_specs.py`, so the extractor's planning step imports no torch (`torch imported: False`, measured 2026-09-21).
- **Implies:** a Sbi-extractor commit that needs `cohort.py` is pushed after the SBI commit that carries it.
- **Sources:** `[KB]` migration handoff S5 (the 09-21 decisions; rows D and D-1), S11.1.
- **Status:** active.

### D-009 -- The DUP15HD simulated campaigns will be re-extracted
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-21 / `[user]` `[STATED, emphatic]`
- **Binds:** the sim arm's detections (`ANN/MEA_analysis/Outputs/` and its successor).
- **Decision:** the simulated campaigns WILL be re-extracted; "the decision stands and is not to be re-argued".
- **Why:** overrides the same-day analysis that re-exporting would suffice (`process_campaign.py` detects spikes only, so no manifest field forces a re-extraction). That analysis is not withdrawn; the reason given on 2026-09-24 is D-012.
- **Implies:** Stage C branch A (C8).
- **Does not decide:** the scope, settled 2026-09-24 by D-011..D-015.
- **Sources:** `[KB]` migration handoff S6 ("DECISION 2026-09-21"); Stage C handoff C5.
- **Status:** active.

### D-010 -- Project naming: DUP15HD vs the Giulia project
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-21 / `[user]` `[STATED]`
- **Binds:** every document and data root.
- **Decision:** the real MEA cohort (`DATA_C` / `DATA_P`, 35 `ptrain_*` wells) and the simulated campaigns are the DUP15HD project; the Giulia project is separate and out of scope. The acronym is not expanded anywhere.
- **Does not decide:** which trees belong to which. That is Claude's inference (`HPC_PATHS.md`, "Project naming"): DUP15HD = `campaign_cadex_rho1300*` under `Phenomenological/Main/` and `MEA_analysis/Outputs/`; Giulia project = `Phenomenological/Main/Giulia_Astro/` (`campaign_cadex_hhgap_*`) and `MEA_analysis/mea_out*/`.
- **Sources:** `[KB]` `HPC_PATHS.md` header; Stage C handoff "Project naming"; migration handoff header.
- **Status:** active.

### D-011 (= SC-D1) -- Re-run the detection over the existing raw simulations
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-24 / `[user]`
- **Binds:** Stage C C5, C8; `process_campaign.py`.
- **Decision:** re-run the virtual-MEA detection, `process_campaign.py`, over the existing raw simulations, with the electrode count taken from the real arm.
- **Statement:** no new simulator sweeps, so theta and the raw spike times do not change; the campaigns are D-013's, `n_e` is D-015's, the output root D-014's; every other detection parameter stays at its current value.
- **Why:** D-012.
- **Implies:** C8 (designed, not built); the sim-arm export then reads `Outputs_v2` as `MEA_ROOT` with `SIM_ROOT` unchanged.
- **Does not decide:** the electrode pitch (see "Open calls").
- **Sources:** `[KB]` Stage C handoff, "Decisions of 2026-09-24", C5, C8.
- **Status:** active.

### D-012 (= SC-D2) -- Why re-run: every file of the new bank made by the new pipeline
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-24 / `[user]`
- **Binds:** the re-extraction's provenance record.
- **Decision:** "it might not change anything", but everything is re-run so that every file of the new bank was produced by the new pipeline and says so.
- **Implies:** C8's root-level record carries the cohort manifest's sha256, the ANN commit and the task list (C8 step 3), and since 2026-09-28 also the EAP library's sha256 and the environment's versions (Stage C handoff v3).
- **Sources:** `[KB]` Stage C handoff, "Decisions of 2026-09-24", C8.
- **Status:** active.

### D-013 (= SC-D3) -- All DUP15HD campaigns
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-24 / `[user]` ("all"); the reading below is Claude's.
- **Binds:** the re-extraction's task list (C8 step 1).
- **Decision:** re-extract all campaigns.
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** every `campaign_cadex_rho1300v{1,2,3,4,5,7,8,9}` under `SIM_MAIN_DIR` -- v9 included although it was never MEA-processed; the Giulia trees excluded.
- **Implies:** C8 step 1 names every task without `manifest.json` and every campaign without raw `topo_*/iter_*.npz`, instead of skipping them.
- **Does not decide:** whether v9 has raw output at all (not on record; probe P5).
- **Sources:** `[KB]` Stage C handoff, "Decisions of 2026-09-24"; `HPC_PATHS.md` 4a, 4g.
- **Status:** active; the reading awaits the user's confirmation (see "Open calls"). 2026-10-01 `[CLUSTER, probe P5/P6]`: v9 has raw output for 8 of its 54 tasks (36,256 `iter_*.npz`; its other 46 sweep folders hold neither `job_args.json` nor `manifest.json`), and it was MEA-processed on 2026-08-21 -- 8 sweep folders in `Outputs/`, 6 of them with `mea_manifest.json` -- so the Statement's "never MEA-processed" is wrong. `campaign_cadex_rho1300v10`, `v11`, `v12` (11 + 11 + 17 tasks, raw output complete, no MEA output) exist under `SIM_MAIN_DIR`, outside the reading's list (see "Open calls"). 2026-10-01: the reading is made explicit and extended to v10-v12 by D-025.

### D-014 (= SC-D4) -- Root `ANN/MEA_analysis/Outputs_v2`
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-24 / `[user, delegated]`
- **Binds:** the re-extraction's output root.
- **Decision:** `/davinci-1/home/ldellamea/ANN/MEA_analysis/Outputs_v2`.
- **Why:** beside the root of record `Outputs/`, which is never overwritten; `_v2` as in `extracted_v2`; no whitespace, so `qsub -v` can carry it.
- **Implies:** `Outputs/` stays the archive of record for the r2-era results. Not created as of 2026-09-28.
- **Sources:** `[KB]` Stage C handoff, "Decisions of 2026-09-24"; `HPC_PATHS.md` 4g.
- **Status:** active. [2026-10-06: the Implies line -- `Outputs/` stays the archive of record for the r2-era results -- is superseded by D-065 once C8's record exists: the old detections of every task the record covers are then deleted.]

### D-015 (= SC-D5) -- The sim arm's `n_e` comes from the cohort manifest
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-24 / `[user]`
- **Binds:** the re-extraction's `--n_side`; the sim-arm export's geometry check.
- **Decision:** set `n_e` from the real-arm manifest at re-extraction: `n_side = sqrt(electrodes_per_subset)` = 3 (manifest value 9). No profiling of the old outputs, since nothing is exported from them any more; `assert_sim_geometry` stays as the export-time gate.
- **Statement:** for the new outputs `n_e` is configured from the manifest, then checked (this replaces C1's "`n_e` can only be checked"). C8 step 1 refuses unless `n_side^2 == electrodes_per_subset`.
- **Does not decide:** the electrode pitch. The manifest records the electrode count per subregion, not the spacing, so `--pitch` stays at 60 um unless decided (see "Open calls").
- **Sources:** `[KB]` Stage C handoff, "Decisions of 2026-09-24", C1, C8.
- **Status:** active. Its open pitch question is settled by D-021 (2026-09-30).

### D-016 (= SC-D6) -- `LABEL_AXES` explicit for every campaign set
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-24 / `[user]`
- **Binds:** `launch_sweep_exports.sh`, `submit_sbi_export.sh`, `example_export.py --mode campaign`.
- **Decision:** `LABEL_AXES` is passed explicitly for every campaign set.
- **Why:** the old default was the r2 weibull file, which trips the NaN guard in `assemble_theta_A` on every job of a flat campaign set.
- **Implies:** commit C2b (Sbi-extractor): the launcher and the worker exit 9 without it; `example_export.py --mode campaign` exits 2 without `--label_axes`; `none` selects the legacy 4-axis block, by name only. Its state: Stage C handoff C2b.

### D-021 -- The virtual probe's pitch mirrors the recording device: 60 um
- **Date / by:** 2026-09-30 / `[user]` (this conversation, 13:08 Europe/Rome)
- **Binds:** `process_campaign.py --pitch` for the C8 re-extraction; `PITCH_UM` in Sbi-extractor `extractor/channel_subset_extraction.py`.
- **Decision (the user's words):** "For now the recordings are done on a device that has fixed 60 um of pitch."
- **Statement:** the virtual MEA's electrode pitch (centre-to-centre) equals the recording device's, 60 um. That is already `process_campaign.py`'s default (`--pitch 60.0`) and the real extractor's `PITCH_UM = 60.0`, so C8 passes no pitch flag. The pitch describes hardware: it is configured to the device, not tuned. The mirroring is Claude's reading of the user's answer, stated in the reply of 2026-09-30. "For now": a different device changes `--pitch` on the sim arm and `PITCH_UM` in the extractor, which uses it only for physical coordinates (its nearest-neighbour ranking works on grid indices).
- **Why:** in the virtual probe the pitch sets the electrode-to-neuron distances, hence which neurons each electrode detects and at what amplitude; parity of the observable's geometry between the arms needs it to match, as `n_e` does (D-015).
- **Implies:** no change for C8. Proposed, not built: C8's completion gate also checks the `pitch` in each `meta_json` against the device's.
- **Does not decide:** the electrode size. `--edge` (default 25 um, the virtual electrode's side length) is a hardware property too; whether the device's electrodes match it was asked on 2026-09-30 (see "Open calls"). Nor does matching the pitch match the footprint: a real subregion is a compact 3x3 only when all its neighbours pass the MFR threshold, otherwise its 9 electrodes spread farther, while the virtual probe is always a compact 3x3 (migration handoff S6).
- **Sources:** `[user]` this conversation, 2026-09-30; `[CODE ANN@73c2fcd]` `hpc/MEA Traces/process_campaign.py:551`, `mea_probe.py:12-13, 49-51`, and `THEORY.md`'s provenance table, which records the probe geometry (3x3, 25 um edge, 60 um pitch) as the user's specification; `[CODE Sbi-extractor@442eaf4]` `extractor/channel_subset_extraction.py:380-392`.
- **Status:** active. Its "Does not decide" on the electrode size is settled by D-024 (2026-10-01).

### D-026 -- Raster figures: one typical well per batch folder
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-01 / `[user]` (this conversation, 12:55 Europe/Rome; chosen among options Claude offered, so the wording is the option's)
- **Binds:** the default well selection of the raster tool (Sbi-extractor `raster/`: `raster_data.select_typical_wells`, `run_raster_plots.py select`); the six panels of the comparison figure.
- **Decision:** "One per batch, typical" -- 3 control + 3 pathological wells, one from each batch folder: the well with the median number of active electrodes (MFR >= 0.1 Hz), read from its extraction record.
- **Statement:** for each class of the cohort manifest's `classes`, and each batch folder of that class (`root_name`, the middle component of a well's `out_dir`), sort the folder's wells by (`n_active`, culture), with `n_active = n_present - len(discarded)` read from the well's `traces_meta.json` -- the size of the extractor's valid set V, MFR counted over the whole recording -- and take the element at index floor((n - 1) / 2), the lower median when n is even. The manifest of record has three batch folders per class (control `DATA_C_Batch3` 5 wells, `Batch4_SubBatch1` 6, `Batch4_SubBatch2` 6; pathological `DATA_P_Batch4`, `Batch3_SubBatch1`, `Batch3_SubBatch2`, 6 each), hence 3 + 3. Naming wells explicitly (`--wells`) overrides the rule, and the outputs then say so.
- **Why:** one well per batch folder spans each class's batch structure; the median keeps an extreme well out; the choice needs no pass over the raw rasters.
- **Implies:** the rule is the tool's default; every figure's JSON sidecar records which rule chose its wells and each well's `n_active`.
- **Does not decide:** that `n_active` (how much of the array is active) is the right meaning of "typical" for every question -- a well can sit at the median of coverage and be atypical in bursting; which windows are shown.
- **Sources:** `[user]` this conversation, 2026-10-01; `[CODE Sbi-extractor@63d2574]` `extractor/run_channel_subset_extraction.py` (what `traces_meta.json` holds), `extractor/channel_subset_extraction.py:689-691` (V and the discarded set); `[KB]` `HPC_PATHS.md` sec. 3b (classes, batch folders, well counts).
- **Status:** active.

### D-027 -- Raster y-axis: the active electrodes, in chip order
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-01 / `[user]` (this conversation, 12:55 Europe/Rome; chosen among offered options)
- **Binds:** the rows of every raster panel (`raster_data.raster_view`), static and interactive.
- **Decision:** "Active electrodes" -- every electrode with MFR >= 0.1 Hz (the cohort's `mfr_threshold`), ordered by chip position: the whole-culture view.
- **Statement (Claude's reading, as offered):** the rows are V = {e present : N_e / T_rec >= mfr_threshold}, with N_e the electrode's spike count over the whole recording (not over the plotted window), T_rec = n_samples_raw / fs_raw, and `mfr_threshold`, `fs_raw`, `index_base`, `grid_width` read from the cohort manifest (0.1 Hz, 10110.09 Hz, 1, 48; D-002); ordered by linear index ascending, i.e. row-major on the 48 x 48 grid, chip row 0 at the top as in the extractor's electrode map. Silent and sub-threshold electrodes are not drawn. V is the set the extraction partitions, and the tool refuses a well whose recomputed sub-threshold set differs from the `discarded` its extraction recorded.
- **Why:** the whole culture without empty rows; the same electrode set the pipeline draws its subregions from.
- **Does not decide:** a figure of only the 81 subregion electrodes the DSN sees, or one ordered by rate (both offered, not chosen).
- **Sources:** `[user]` this conversation, 2026-10-01; `[CODE Sbi-extractor@63d2574]` `extractor/channel_subset_extraction.py:481-499` (MFR), `:689-691` (V), `extractor/channel_subset_viz.py` (row 0 at the top).
- **Status:** active.

### D-028 -- Raster time window: command-line windows and an interactive viewer
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-01 / `[user]` (this conversation, 12:55 Europe/Rome; chosen among offered options)
- **Binds:** `run_raster_plots.py plot --window` and `run_raster_plots.py view` (`raster/raster_viewer.py`).
- **Decision:** "Both" -- `--window T0 T1` (seconds, repeatable) for static PNG/PDF figures, plus an interactive viewer with a range slider and a Save button.
- **Statement:** a window is the half-open interval [T0, T1) in seconds from the start of the recording, 0 <= T0 < T1 <= T_rec; a spike at raw sample s (0-based) sits at s / fs_raw. The viewer saves through the same drawing code as `plot`, so a saved window and a `--window` figure of the same span are the same figure. With no `--window`: [0, 60) s `[Claude's default, a flag]`.
- **Implies:** the viewer needs a display (X11 forwarding, e.g. MobaXterm's) and refuses to start without one.
- **Does not decide:** which windows go into a paper or a deck.
- **Sources:** `[user]` this conversation, 2026-10-01.
- **Status:** active.

### D-033 -- "Exporting the new simulations" meant the re-extraction alone: Stage C ends at its record
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-01 / `[user]` (this conversation, 14:22 Europe/Rome), answering the question Claude had put in the previous reply: was the embedding export of the re-extracted simulations wanted now, or did "exporting" mean the re-extraction alone.
- **Binds:** the scope of Stage C (status rows A3 and J of the Stage C handoff); where I-001 starts; the Open-calls row on a provisional export with r2 (closed by this entry).
- **Decision (the user's words, spelling corrected):** "It is just re-extraction given the new environment."
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** by "exporting the new simulations" (D-032) the user meant running the raw simulations through the extraction again -- C8, `process_campaign.py` over v1-v5, v7-v12 into `Outputs_v2` -- and not the embedding export (`launch_sweep_exports.sh`: detections -> pooled IFR -> frozen DSN -> Parquet shards). So Stage C ends at C8's completion gate and its record, `Outputs_v2/REEXTRACTION_RECORD.json`; no export is made with the r2 encoder before the retrain; the embedding export of the new simulations waits for the retrain (D-031) and the encoder it produces. "The new environment" is read as `sbi_export` (D-023), the env C8 runs in; it could also mean the new pipeline as a whole, and nothing here depends on which.
- **Why:** not stated. `[reasoning]` (Claude's, not the user's) the embedding export needs a frozen encoder, and the only one on hand, r2, was trained under `meacnn_cpu` and is not carried forward (D-003); an export with it would be redone after the retrain.

### D-038 -- Fix patches are prepared for the space/runner parity findings F-a and F-b
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-01 / `[user]` (the documentation chat, about 15:00 Europe/Rome; chosen among two options on a question card: "Also prepare fix patches").
- **Binds:** `hpc/joint/stage4/npe_tune_joint.py` (`AXIS_TO_FLAG`, `build_argv`, the `spec_fixed` handling); `hpc/joint/stage3/run_joint_arms.py` (`--num-bins`, a flag for `head_pool_ops`, `make_backbone`); `hpc/joint/stage4/joint_space.py` (`default_joint_space().fixed`); the Stage 3 and Stage 4 smoke suites.
- **Decision:** besides being reported in Set P, the two findings get fix patches with smoke tests, built through the `hpc-git-delivery` gates: F-a -- the runner's `--num-bins` default is 8 (`run_joint_arms.py:229`), `build_joint_model`'s is 10, the space declares `num_bins` FIXED at 10, and `build_argv` passes no `--num-bins`, so a Stage 4 trial trains an 8-bin flow while its ledger spec says 10; F-b -- the space declares `head_pool_ops` fixed at code 1 (`("mean", "max", "std")`), `make_backbone` never sets it, so `BackboneConfig`'s default `("mean",)` (code 0) is built `[REPO 834eb41]`.
- **Statement (Claude's reading of what any fix must preserve):** the ledger must record the configuration that trained. The tuner therefore passes the space's fixed values to the runner (`--num-bins` from `spec.fixed["num_bins"]`; a new `--head-pool-ops` flag from `spec.fixed["head_pool_ops"]`, decoded by `condition_space.decode_head_pool_ops`), and the runner forwards `head_pool_ops` into `BackboneConfig`. Whether the runner's own defaults for Stage 3 move to the space's values (8 -> 10; code 0 -> 1) is a separate design choice: it changes what the Stage 3 arms train at "default hyper-parameters" (plan Stage 3), and it is NOT decided here.
- **Why:** not stated. The finding's consequence -- a ledger recording a cell that did not run -- is what was raised.
- **Implies:** a new stage in the plan (v1.1) for the patch stream, separate from the documents: a branch of its own, each patch with its smoke test (the runner's R0e/R0g-style regression that an invocation passing no flag behaves as before; a tuner test that `build_argv` emits both flags and that the ledger config and the runner's received config agree), gates 1-6, and a dry run on the cluster before any submission. After it lands, `JOINT_DSN_NPE_USAGE_v1.md` S5.2 (the recognised `-v` variables and the "reachable only by editing the script" list) and the plan's S5.1 fixed list get a note.
- **Does not decide:** the alignment direction for the Stage 3 defaults (above); when the patches are applied; whether the minor findings F-c (the `weight_decay` provenance string), F-d (`patience=99`), F-e (the stale `n_posterior_draws` docstring), F-f (the stale margin docstring) also get fixes -- not asked (Open calls).
- **Sources:** `[user]` this conversation, 2026-10-01; plan S4 (F-a, F-b), S9 (Q8); `[REPO 834eb41]` `run_joint_arms.py:229, 301-308`, `joint_model.py:195-197`, `joint_space.py:322-324`, `npe_tune_joint.py:86-117, 198-203`, `hpc/dsn/backbone.py:76`, `hpc/dsn/condition_space.py:113, 263`.

### D-043 -- One electrode per subregion, nine subregions per well
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-01 / `[user]` (this conversation, 15:35 Europe/Rome), answering Q1 and Q3.
- **Binds:** the Giulia cohort block (`electrodes_per_subset` 1, `n_subsets` 9); the sim-arm `n_side` (= 1, D-015's rule); `n_e` = 1 on both arms; `n_units` = 9 x the number of wells kept.
- **Decision (the user's words):** "The simulated cultures are narrow thus even though it is risky we have to extract those recordings with just one electrode. Let's draw then 9 electrodes from each recorded well."
- **Statement:** E = `electrodes_per_subset` = 1 and C = `n_subsets` = 9 on the real arm; `n_side` = 1 on the simulated arm, one virtual electrode at the centre of the 300 um culture. Which nine electrodes: the extractor's rule -- the nine electrodes of highest mean firing rate among those at or above `mfr_threshold`, ties to the lower index (`channel_subset_extraction.py:641-737`); with E = 1 no neighbour is searched, so the grid geometry does not enter the choice. That "draw" means this ranked choice and not a random draw is Claude's reading `[to confirm]`; a random draw would be a code change. A well with fewer than nine electrodes at or above the threshold is discarded by the extractor (`InsufficientElectrodesError`), as designed.
- **Why:** at 200 um pitch only one electrode fits inside the 300 um simulated culture (a centred 3 x 3 leaves 8 of 9 outside, a 2 x 2 fits but the real extractor forms a T, not a square: Giulia handoff v1.1, Q3 `[SANDBOX 2026-10-01]`); the user accepts the risk of a one-electrode observable.
- **Implies:** every subregion trace on both arms is one electrode's IFR; the real nine electrodes spread over the chip while the virtual one sits at the culture centre (a footprint difference the user accepts).
- **Does not decide:** the selection rule beyond the extractor's existing one; a minimum number of wells per condition.
- **Sources:** `[user]` this conversation, 2026-10-01 15:35; `[CODE Sbi-extractor@63d2574]` `extractor/channel_subset_extraction.py:641-737`; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-01]` the geometry check of the Giulia handoff v1.1.

With `n_side` 1 the probe's `--pitch` (200) does not move it: the centre offsets are (i - (n_side - 1)/2) x pitch = 0 (`mea_probe.py:76-83` `[CODE ANN@d0ced88]`). File sizes run from 242 bytes to 94 KB; 13 files are exactly 242 bytes.
- **Why:** where the user put the data; the conditions as the user named them.
- **Implies:** (i) `class_roots` = the four folders above, two for class 0; `well_glob` `ptrain_*` matches the well folders and nothing else; `root_name_for` gives `Batch1_100N0A_wo`, `Batch2_100N0A_wo`, `AraC_50N50A`, `AraC_70N30A`, all distinct, so `{root_name}__{well}` is unique. (ii) The files are NOT in the format the extractor reads -- `ptrain_<k>.mat`, one dense binary raster `ptrain` of shape (n_samples, 1) (`channel_subset_extraction.py:118-119, 211-282`): the names do not match `^ptrain_(\d+)\.mat$`, so `load_ptrain_folder` would find no file and every array task would abort; and the sizes rule out dense 15-min rasters at 10 kHz, since a 9 000 000-sample all-zero `uint8` raster saved compressed is 8 951 bytes `[SANDBOX 2026-10-01, scipy.io.savemat]` while a silent channel here is 242 bytes, and an empty sparse (9 000 000, 1) double column saved uncompressed is 224 bytes, 7 800 spikes in one 93 824 bytes `[SANDBOX, same]`, against the listing's 242 bytes and 94 KB. So the files are read as sparse (or index-list) spike trains whose variable name and storage class the read-only probe (plan B2a, `extractor/probe_ptrain_tree.py`) reports; the loader gains a declared `ptrain_format` with `ptrain_varname` and `ptrain_name_pattern` (plan B2b), set from the probe's output, never guessed at load time. Claude's recollection, NOT verified: the naming is the SpyCode convention (Bologna et al. 2010, Neural Netw, DOI 10.1016/j.neunet.2010.05.002 -- PubMed, abstract only; no PMC full text; the abstract says nothing about file formats), whose peak trains are sparse arrays holding the spike amplitude at the spike sample under the variable `peak_train`. Nothing is configured on that recollection.
- **Does not decide:** the file format (the probe); the well-exclusion rule when fewer than 9 x E electrodes reach `mfr_threshold` -- the AraC wells have 17 to 35 files, and a well below the count ends in `InsufficientElectrodesError`, which under the manifest's rules (D-002: a fragment per listed well) refuses the whole cohort. Claude proposes an `exclude_wells` list in the cohort block, filled from the probe's census and recorded in the manifest (Open calls). A minimum number of wells per condition (four AraC wells per condition is what there is).
- **Sources:** `[user]` this conversation, 2026-10-01 about 16:10; `[LISTING 2026-10-01]` the attached listing; `[CODE Sbi-extractor@63d2574]` `extractor/channel_subset_extraction.py:118-119, 211-282, 332-343`; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-01]` the `savemat` size measurements; `[PubMed, abstract only]` Bologna LL et al., Neural Netw 2010, DOI 10.1016/j.neunet.2010.05.002.

### D-048 -- `fs_raw` is 10 000 Hz exactly; grid positions are numbered from 1; the row/column code is decoded with `grid_width` 10, `index_base` 0
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-01 / `[user]` (this conversation, about 16:10 Europe/Rome), answering P3 and P4. The decoding is Claude's reading of the user's statement against the listing.
- **Binds:** the Giulia cohort block (`fs_raw`, `grid_width`, `index_base`); `validate_grid` and the electrode-map figure for the Giulia wells; the probe's T_rec check.
- **Decision (the user's words, spelling corrected):** "P3/4) exact, grid positions start from 1, I do not know how many electrodes there are, it doesn't matter because we use just one electrode for the extraction."
- **Statement:** `fs_raw` = 10000.0 Hz, so T_rec = n_samples / 10000 s and a 15-min recording holds n_samples = 9 000 000 (the probe prints T_rec per well and flags a well whose AraC recording is not 900 s; the `0:100` lengths are variable, D-040). The file code `<rc>` (D-047) is a position code: `<rc>` = 10 * row + col with row, col in 1..8, the positions numbered from 1 as the user says `[the listing confirms: 012..087, no 0 digit, no 9 digit]`. Two senses of "index base" are live here and are kept apart: the user's "grid positions start from 1" is the numbering of rows and columns; the config field `index_base` is the extractor's offset, subtracted from the file integer before its row-major decoding `row = (k - index_base) // grid_width`, `col = (k - index_base) % grid_width` (`channel_subset_extraction.py:406-413`). The position code is decoded exactly by `grid_width` = 10 and `index_base` = 0: k = 12 -> (1, 2), k = 87 -> (8, 7), every one of the 60 codes inside the 10 x 10 range check `[SANDBOX 2026-10-01]`. That is a decoding convention, not a claim that the chip has 100 electrodes: rows and columns 0 and 9 are never used. `grid_width` = 8 with `index_base` = 1 would decode k = 12 as (1, 3), wrong. With E = 1 (D-043) the (row, col) enter only `validate_grid` and the map figure; the pitch (200 um, D-042) enters nothing in the extraction (`PITCH_UM` is used by `electrode_coords` alone, which the extraction does not call).
- **Why:** the device's rate; the files' naming.
- **Implies:** the Giulia cohort block states `fs_raw 10000.0, grid_width 10, index_base 0`, and the plan's first check prints the decoded (row, col) of the 60 codes. The DUP15HD block (`grid_width 48, index_base 1`) is untouched.
- **Does not decide:** nothing further.
- **Sources:** `[user]` this conversation, 2026-10-01 about 16:10; `[LISTING 2026-10-01]`; `[CODE Sbi-extractor@63d2574]` `extractor/channel_subset_extraction.py:406-478`; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-01]` the decoding check (`smoke_test_ptrain_formats.py`).
- **Status:** active. Settles D-042's exact rate and grid mapping.

### D-055 -- Two Giulia wells below nine active electrodes are excluded by name; the cohort is 16 wells
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-02 / `[user]` (the Giulia chat, 13:12 Europe/Rome), choosing among the three options of the reply of 10:32 -- exclude the two wells by name (Claude's default), lower `mfr_threshold` for the whole cohort, or form fewer than nine subregions in every well -- with the three outputs that reply asked for attached.
- **Binds:** `cohort.exclude_wells` of `hpc/dsn/hpc/Config/config_giulia_cohort.davinci.json` (SBI); the extraction manifest `extraction_manifest_giulia.tsv` and the Stage D array (16 tasks); the Giulia cohort manifest's `excluded_wells`, `n_wells` and `n_units`; every downstream count per class (class 0 now 8 wells).
- **Decision (the user's words):** "It's ok if some wells are excluded because there is no activity".
- **Statement:** `exclude_wells` = [`ptrain_44456_DIV35_100N0A_nbasal_0001`, `ptrain_45198_DIV35_100N0A_nbasal_0001`]: the two wells whose valid set V -- the electrodes whose mean firing rate over the whole recording is at or above `mfr_threshold` = 0.1 Hz (D-044) -- holds 1 and 5 electrodes, against the C x E = 9 x 1 = 9 the extraction needs (D-043) `[CLUSTER 2026-10-02: the probe's census and the two failed tasks' InsufficientElectrodesError lines, which agree]`. Both are in `Batch1/100N0A_wo` (class 0, 0:100, untreated). The wells are not silent -- 1 and 5 electrodes reach the threshold -- so "no activity" is read as too little activity to form nine single-electrode subregions `[Claude's reading]`. The exclusion is by name, in the config; the cohort manifest records the names under `excluded_wells` (D-002: a listed well without a fragment refuses the cohort). `mfr_threshold`, `n_subsets` and `electrodes_per_subset` are unchanged, so no kept well's nine electrodes change. The cohort is 16 wells, 8 / 4 / 4 by class, `n_units` = 16 x 9 = 144.
- **Why:** with E = 1 and C = 9 the extractor needs nine electrodes in V and refuses a well with fewer. The two alternatives act on every well: a lower threshold changes the cohort's recorded `mfr_threshold` (D-044) and every well's V, though not the nine electrodes of a well that already passes; fewer subregions changes `n_subsets` for the whole cohort, which the manifest asserts constant across wells (D-002).
- **Implies:** the SBI config commit of 2026-10-02 sets the list; before the re-run the probe with `--config` must end `PROBE OK; CONFIG MATCHES (16 wells to extract)`; the first run's root `extracted_giulia/` (16 complete wells, two empty folders) is moved aside, not deleted, since the launcher refuses a non-empty declared root; the aggregation's PASS line is `wrote .../cohort_manifest.json  sha256 ...`, with `n_wells` 16, `n_units` 144 and `excluded_wells` the two names. For a later Giulia recording, a well enters `exclude_wells` when the probe's census flags it `too_few_active` -- Claude's reading of "some wells", to be confirmed when it next happens.
- **Does not decide:** (i) whether removing the two least active wells of class 0 biases the comparison across conditions -- the exclusion selects on activity, the quantity the project compares; (ii) class 0's batch structure: the three kept Batch1 wells have 9, 11 and 9 electrodes at or above 0.1 Hz, the five Batch2 wells 32 to 59 `[CLUSTER 2026-10-02, the probe's census]`, so each Batch1 well's nine electrodes are nearly all of its V -- whether and how the downstream statistics account for batch (Open calls, the Giulia row's (g)); (iii) any activity floor per well beyond the extractor's nine electrodes (none is set).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the Giulia chat, 2026-10-02 13:12; `[CLUSTER 2026-10-02]` the user's paste of `out/cohort_manifest_giulia.log` (REFUSED: 2 of 18 wells), `out/chsub_mea_array_giulia_3.log` and `_4.log` (`formed only 1 of 9 requested subregions (size E=1) from |V|=1`; `formed only 5 of 9 ... |V|=5`) and `out/probe_giulia.log` (verdicts `ok` 16, `too_few_active` 2; its suggested `exclude_wells` the same two names); D-002, D-043, D-044; `[CODE SBI@ab7ea82]` `hpc/dsn/cohort.py` (`exclude_wells`: validated as folder names, applied at listing time).

### D-056 -- Real-arm windows: 180 s, back to back, from t = 0
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-02 / `[user]` (the Giulia chat, 15:09 Europe/Rome), answering the open stride question (Open calls, the Giulia row's (f); plan O6), which the reply of 13:50 had raised.
- **Binds:** the tiling of every Giulia real recording into windows: the real mode of the window exporter (plan B5), the real-arm Stage 1 bank (G4), and the windows the posteriors and the replicate statistic see (G6). Not the simulated arm, which has one 180 s window per 180 s simulation (D-041).
- **Decision (the user's words):** "The step between windows is still open. I would do 180 seconds with zero seconds lag however."
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** windows of W_s = 180 s at stride S = 180 s: consecutive windows abut, with no gap and no overlap, and the first starts at t = 0 ("zero seconds lag"; read as no offset at the start instead, it gives the same tiling). For a trace of length T_rec the windows are [k S, k S + W_s) for k = 0, ..., n - 1, with n = floor((T_rec - W_s) / S) + 1 -- the tiling rule of `hpc/dsn/data_splits.py::window_starts`. At the measured lengths (D-040's Status): a 0:100 trace (300 s) gives one window, [0, 180) s, and leaves [180, 300) s -- 120 s, 40 % of the trace -- unused; an AraC trace (600 s) gives three, [0, 180), [180, 360), [360, 540) s, and leaves [540, 600) s -- 60 s, 10 % -- unused. With the 16 wells of D-055 and nine units per well: 8 x 9 x 1 = 72 windows for class 0 and 4 x 9 x 3 = 108 for each AraC class, 288 in all `[reasoning, from the tiling rule]`.
- **Why:** not stated. `[reasoning, Claude's]` non-overlapping windows share no samples, so two windows of one trace are not partial copies of each other.
- **Implies:** B5's real mode tiles at S = 180 s from t = 0 (its row in the plan already says "windows of 180 s at stride 180 s"); D-041's default becomes a decision. The classes contribute unequal numbers of windows (72 against 108), which matters to anything that pools windows across classes.
- **Does not decide:** whether the unused tails are used in another way (a window aligned to the end of a recording, say); whether the windows of one trace count as independent replicates or as repeated measures in the per-condition statistics (G6); anything on the simulated arm.
- **Sources:** `[user]` the Giulia chat, 2026-10-02 15:09; D-040 (Status), D-041; `[CODE SBI@ab7ea82]` `hpc/dsn/data_splits.py::window_starts` (start 0, then every S while the window fits).
- **Status:** active. Settles D-041's "Does not decide" on the real-arm stride and, by the reading above, on the first window (no offset). [2026-10-16 12:08 `[CLUSTER, the pipeline log pasted at 12:29]`: the real bank of record, built by the pipeline (`launch_bank.sh real`) -- 100N0A_wo 8 wells, 72 windows, 6 below the 0.1 Hz floor (kept and counted, D-075); 50N50A 4 wells, 108 windows, 0 below; 70N30A 4 wells, 108 windows, 0 below; `wrote ~/banks/giulia_real/shard_0000.npz rows 288 sha256 14892c3057b528ce contract f306f8d121ee98f4` -- 72 + 108 + 108 = 288 windows over the 16 wells, as this rule gives for their lengths.]

- **Date / by:** 2026-10-03 / `[user]` (the Giulia chat, 15:25 Europe/Rome), answering Claude's question of the reply before: "Before you run it: is any simulation still running, in v12 or elsewhere?"

Everything else of D-059 stands (v1-v5, v7-v12, over the mixed contract). "The campaigns available" is read as the finished simulations; the two alternatives were not taken: waiting for the task contradicts "for now", and running it half-written risks a record that calls it complete -- the plan cannot tell an unfinished simulation from a finished one with fewer iterations (it counts `iter_*.npz`, `sim_reextract_plan.py:203-222`), and the gate refuses a task whose raw iterations grew only if a new one was written between the array's plan and the gate (`sim_reextract_gate.py:21-34`) `[CODE Sbi-extractor@0a560ca]`.

Expected on davinci `[reasoning, from the plan of 14:20]`: 254 runnable tasks less the one left out = 253 in the record, the test task kept as done, 252 to run (array 0-251), 48 excluded. Once the record exists, the plan refuses `Outputs_v2` as a finished root (`sim_reextract_plan.py:424`), so the left-out task and I-002's campaigns need a C8 pass of their own -- another root and record, or a mechanism that extends a record -- neither built.

- **Sources:** `[user]` the Giulia chat, 2026-10-03 15:25; D-025, D-059; `[CODE Sbi-extractor@0a560ca]` as cited; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-03]` the suite 25/25 on the patched tree (five runs), two mutations of the option each caught by the suite, the run sheet rehearsed 18/18 on a replica with a task being written.

What is not built is a record that names the record it replaces. Also: the suite ran 25/25 six times on patches with the same code, not five.] [2026-10-05 `[GITHUB]`: the commit is on origin -- Sbi-extractor `feat/real-arm-parity` head `6173e92` (committed 2026-10-03 16:01) has the tested tree `baeaa95fb977d53879b30a71df69547a9f4f3273`, on top of the suite-fix commit `5682a6c`; the "not yet pushed" in Binds is out of date.] [2026-10-06: its left-out task `v12/sweep_cfd_task0010` has finished and goes into the C8 re-run, with `v12/sweep_cfd_task0011` (D-068); the re-plan runs with no task left out by name.] [corrected 2026-10-07: the Statement's "the gate refuses a task whose raw iterations grew only if a new one was written between the array's plan and the gate (`sim_reextract_gate.py:21-34`)" misstates what the gate checks. It never re-counts the raw folders: it compares each task's MEA output with the plan's counts, `iters_per_topo` and `n_iters` (`sim_reextract_gate.py:112, 146-163` `[CODE Sbi-extractor@1b23adc]`). So it refuses a task whose raw folder grew between the plan and the start of that task's MEA job, which processes what it finds -- as for `v12/sweep_cfd_task0011` on 2026-10-06 (D-068's Status) -- and it does not see growth after that start: such a task passes with the plan's count. The Statement's conclusion, leave a running simulation out, stands, with more reason.] [corrected 2026-10-07, about 13:10: "between the plan and the start of that task's MEA job" and "growth after that start" above are too coarse. The MEA job lists the `topo_*` folders when it starts and each topology's `iter_*.npz` when its worker reaches that topology (`process_campaign.py:297`, in the C8-era copy `[CODE ANN@04977e0]`); with one worker the topologies run in turn. So the gate catches growth up to the moment the job lists each topology -- task0011's `topo_00008` was listed about 2.5 h after the job started, when it held 192 files -- and misses growth after it.]

### D-061 -- C8 does not re-detect a task that replays another task's seed (DUP15HD)
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-03 / `[user]` (the Giulia chat, 16:08 Europe/Rome), with the output of the C8 dry run (S7x) attached, before the array was submitted.
- **Binds:** the task list of the first C8 run (narrows D-013, D-025, D-059, D-060: "all" runnable tasks becomes one task per seed); the array (C8 handoff P10, S8x, held until this is applied); the C8 plan (`sim_reextract/sim_reextract_plan.py`), which gets a replay gate; the record; the Giulia version of the machinery (B3, D-045), which needs the same gate.
- **Decision (the user's words, spelling corrected):** "Be aware that there are campaigns with the same seed, especially within the first ones. Do not extract that. Is there a gate that filters them before extraction?"
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** the DUP15HD counterpart of D-045. A sweep task whose seed equals another task's, and whose simulations are the other's -- the same theta in the iteration files the two share -- is a replay and is not re-detected: one task per seed. Same seed with different theta is not a replay (different simulations) and is kept, and reported. Where the seed sits in the rho1300 `job_args.json` is not known here: the hhgap campaigns carry `_resolved_seed_master` (`dataset_profile.py:499-504`, `preflight_giulia_export.py:530-535` `[CODE Sbi-extractor@0a560ca]`), and whether v1-v9 (manifest version 3) carry it is NOT VERIFIED -- the read-only census of the C8 handoff (P10, v1.10) reports every `job_args.json` key containing "seed", groups the tasks by those values, and classifies each shared-seed pair (same files, a subset, a superset, different theta, unreadable). There is no gate today: the C8 plan reads no seed `[CODE Sbi-extractor@baeaa95f]`.
- **Why:** the user's: replays are the same simulations twice and add nothing to the bank but duplicate rows; D-045's reasoning, now for DUP15HD. Downstream, the r2-era loader de-duplicated on distinct theta, keep-first `[KB, SBI_PIPELINE.md sec. 5, as cited in EXTRACTOR_USAGE.md 5.3]`, which drops duplicate rows after the work, not before it.
- **Implies:** S8x is not run until the replays are out of the plan. Then a gate in the plan: on by default (this decision is its default), every dropped task named under `excluded` with the task it replays and the seed, a same-seed pair with different theta reported and kept, and an opt-out flag for comparison only; suite checks for each case; through the laptop as before; the same gate serves B3 for the hhgap campaigns (D-045). Until the gate exists, the replays the census names could be left out with `--exclude-task` instead, at the cost of a generic reason in the record.
- **Does not decide:** which copy of a replayed pair is kept. Claude's proposal, to confirm once the census is in: the copy with more iterations (a shorter replay's files are a subset of the longer's); between copies with the same files, the earliest campaign by version number, then the lower task index. Nor what to do if the census shows the seed is a campaign-level value shared by every task of a campaign (then the identity check, not the seed, separates replays from the rest).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the Giulia chat, 2026-10-03 16:08; D-013, D-025, D-045, D-059, D-060; `[CLUSTER 2026-10-03 16:08]` the S7x output; `[CODE]` as cited.
- **Status:** active; the census and the gate pending. [2026-10-05: the gate is built (D-063) -- sandbox commit `ba62f2f` on `6173e92`, tree `39643844715788ec5920ca0388ddc60ae9a7fe52`, delivered as `c8_replay_gate.patch` with the run sheet L2, S3y, P, S7y, S8y, S9y (`claude/HANDOFF_2026-10-01_c8_push_and_run.md` v1.11, P10); not on GitHub yet. The seed key is `_resolved_seed_master` with the offsets `[CODE ANN@04977e0 hpc/Phenomenological_finalv1/HPC_main_sweep.py:1505-1506]`; the C1 census is superseded by S7y's printout of every same-seed group, before anything is submitted.] [2026-10-05 14:00 `[CLUSTER]`, the census C1 on davinci: every one of the 255 tasks with `job_args.json` carries `_resolved_seed_master` and the offsets; 47 seeds shared by 208 tasks of v1-v9, no shared-seed pair with different theta; the test task `v1/sweep_cpu_task0000` a subset of `v9/sweep_cpu_task0000` (log v1.31). ### D-063 -- The replay gate keeps the copy with the most iterations and compares 10 shared files per pair
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-05 / `[user]` (the C8 chat, about 12:50 Europe/Rome; chosen on a question card among options Claude offered, so the wording is the options': "Most iterations (Recommended)", "10 spread files (Recommended)").
- **Binds:** the replay gate of `sim_reextract/sim_reextract_plan.py` (`keeper_order`, `REPLAY_SAMPLE`): sandbox commit `ba62f2f` on Sbi-extractor `6173e92`, tree `39643844715788ec5920ca0388ddc60ae9a7fe52`, delivered as `c8_replay_gate.patch` (47,284 bytes, sha256 `8466616e118860c7dcb5c610c46a25c595ce796449bdbcf14b3a82c692340f06`); the task list and the record of the first C8 run; B3 when it reuses the gate (D-045).
- **Decision:** (1) of a group of same-seed replays the copy with the most iterations is kept; on a tie, the lower campaign version, then the lower task index. (2) Two tasks are compared, theta and params byte for byte, at up to 10 shared iteration files spread from the first to the last.
- **Statement:** (1) settles D-061's "Does not decide" on which copy is kept, as proposed there. (2) is a sample: D-061's "the same theta in the iteration files the two share" is checked at ten files per pair (the first, the last and eight between), not at all of them; the plan and the record say per pair how many shared files were checked. Offered and not chosen: keep the earliest campaign (a longer later copy then holds files the earlier one lacks, so both stay and their shared simulations are re-detected twice); decide per group from a `--keep-replays` printout; compare every shared file (it reads every shared iteration file on every launcher run).
- **Why:** the options' rationale, not stated further by the user: a shorter replay's files are a subset of the longer copy's, so keeping the longest drops every duplicate and loses no simulation; ten files per pair are fast on GPFS.
- **Implies:** the gate as built: tasks with the same seed values (`_resolved_seed_master` and the offsets; the CLI `seed_master` ignored beside it) and the same contract signature form a group; a task is dropped when its iteration files are the same as or a subset of an earlier-kept task's and theta and params are byte-identical at the sampled files; everything else of a group is kept and printed with its relation. The C1 census is not needed before the run: the plan's dry run (S7y) prints every same-seed group with each task's role before anything is submitted. Checked `[SANDBOX 2026-10-05]`: the C8 suite 26/26 twice; five mutations of the gate each failed check P7 alone; the run sheet 13/13 twice on a replica of the laptop clone and of davinci's paths. The commit goes through the laptop (L2): Sbi-extractor is not covered by D-062.
- **Does not decide:** what to do if S7y finds the test task itself a replay of a longer copy (the plan refuses and names the folder to move aside); the hhgap version for B3.
- **Sources:** `[user]` the C8 chat, 2026-10-05; D-061; `[CODE Sbi-extractor sandbox ba62f2f]`; `[CODE ANN@04977e0]` `hpc/Phenomenological_finalv1/HPC_main_sweep.py:1419-1428, 1505-1506`; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-05]`.
- **Status:** active; built, not yet on GitHub (2026-10-05). [2026-10-05 about 14:10: its patch is superseded by v2 (D-064) -- `c8_replay_gate_v2.patch`, tree `67133a86a70943bc8083629ba18fb186c1333e58`, sandbox commit `044fafc`; the keeper order and the 10-file sample are unchanged.] [corrected 2026-10-05 about 15:25 `[GITHUB]`: "not yet on GitHub" above is superseded -- v2 is on `feat/real-arm-parity` since 14:38, head `a8e9a43`, tree `67133a86a70943bc8083629ba18fb186c1333e58`.] [2026-10-05 15:10 `[CLUSTER]`: this entry's last "Does not decide" came up: S7y found the test task `v1/sweep_cpu_task0000` a replay of the kept `v9/sweep_cpu_task0000` (3,504 iterations; the census), refused and named the folder, and the user moved its output out of `Outputs_v2` to `Outputs_v2_replays_moved/campaign_cadex_rho1300v1/sweep_cpu_task0000` (M1). The keeper order on the real data, computed by Claude from the pasted lines: 47 groups, kept by campaign v1 7, v2 4, v3 8, v5 20, v9 8 (112,856 iterations); ties in iteration count fell to the lower campaign version, as for `sweep_intel_task0005` (1,680 in v1 and in v3) and `sweep_intel_task0008` (1,444 in v1 and in v2).]

The two sides of a pair are therefore two simulations of one parameter draw that differ in `O_N` alone (checked at each pair's first shared file; at its other files inferred), not two draws from one prior. ### D-071 -- The MEA re-detection jobs use every core of their node
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-06 / `[user]` (the C8 chat, question card of about 15:55; the user's own text instead of the offered options, "Fix it, after C8's record (Recommended)" and "Keep 1 worker": "Just make it use all the cores").
- **Binds:** Astro-Neuron-Network `hpc/MEA Traces/submit_mea_array.sh` (the worker count) and its copy in `ANN/MEA_analysis/` (D-022); Sbi-extractor `sim_reextract_plan.py` `KNOWN_TOOLS` / `REQUIRED_TOOLS_LABEL`; G2 and any later C8 pass.
- **Decision:** each array task runs one `process_campaign.py` worker per core it was given, not one.
- **Statement (Claude's reading, as built):** the worker count is `PBS_NCPUS` if set, else PBS Pro's `NCPUS`, else `nproc` inside a PBS job, else 1 (a non-integer: a WARNING and 1); BLAS, OpenMP and numexpr are pinned to 1 thread per worker whatever `OMP_NUM_THREADS` PBS set; the log line names the source (`[mea-array] workers  : 48 (from NCPUS)`). `process_campaign.py` gives each topology one worker, so a task gains at most its number of topologies. Before: `NCPUS="${PBS_NCPUS:-1}"`, and `PBS_NCPUS` (a TORQUE name) is not set on davinci, so every task of the 2026-10-05 C8 run used 1 of its 48 cores `[CLUSTER 2026-10-06, D1]`.
- **Why:** the user's. `[reasoning]` G2 is about 1 million simulations by September's count (980,959 in `mea_out_1electrode`, `[KB, HPC_PATHS.md 4f]`) against the 276,347 iterations of the C8 run.
- **Implies:** (1) built and checked in the sandbox `[SANDBOX 2026-10-06]`: ANN sandbox commit `6d4f863` on `04977e0` (information only), subtree `hpc/MEA Traces` `207be0cb821f3d582315571300f0c17e70e287c2`, four files, +305 -8: the job script, `smoke_test_mea_env.py` (check E9; 15 checks), the new `smoke_test_mea_workers.py` (5 checks: `--workers 1` and `3` give bit-identical `mea_iter` files and manifests at the DUP15HD and at the Giulia geometry, `OMP_NUM_THREADS` 1 and 2 likewise, and a one-knob change is caught), the README. The old script, given `NCPUS=48 OMP_NUM_THREADS=48`, passes `--workers 1` with OMP 48 -- the davinci log line -- and the new one `--workers 48` with 1 thread. (2) The change reaches `ANN/MEA_analysis/` only after the C8 record: the C8 gate re-fingerprints the seven tool files and refuses if they changed (`sim_reextract_gate.py:217-224` `[CODE Sbi-extractor@1b23adc]`). (3) `KNOWN_TOOLS` gets the new state as the required one, in the B3 commit. (4) That davinci sets `NCPUS` is not verified: `OMP_NUM_THREADS=48` in the C8 jobs `[CLUSTER 2026-10-06, D1]` fits PBS Pro setting both from `ncpus` `[OpenPBS community forum, read 2026-10-06]`; the G2 test task's workers line is the check, and without `NCPUS` the `nproc` branch applies.
- **Does not decide:** G2's walltime (from the test task); a memory request per job (none is made today; the test task shows the use at 48 workers).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the C8 chat, 2026-10-06; `[CODE ANN@04977e0]` `hpc/MEA Traces/submit_mea_array.sh:216`, `process_campaign.py:44-70, 380-410`; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-06]`; `[web]` community.openpbs.org thread 2843; PubMed queried for the PBS behaviour, rate-limited twice, no result.
- **Status:** active; built in the sandbox, not delivered. ### D-072 -- From G2 on, each simulation's recording noise is seeded from its own `seed_run`; C8's record stays as it is
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-06 / `[user]` (the C8 chat; a question card asked at 16:36 and answered at 16:55 Europe/Rome; the option Claude recommended, "Per simulation (Recommended)", so the wording is the option's).
- **Binds:** Astro-Neuron-Network `hpc/MEA Traces/process_campaign.py` (`noise_entropy`, `noise_seed`, `--noise_seed_scheme`, the per-file `noise_entropy`) and its copy in `ANN/MEA_analysis/` (D-022); Sbi-extractor `sim_reextract_plan.py` (`TOOLS_NOISE_SCHEME`, the plan's `geometry.noise_seed_scheme`) and `sim_reextract_gate.py` (its check); G2 and any later pass with these tools.
- **Decision (the option's words):** "The seed also takes the simulation's own seed_run (a separate draw per simulation in the sweep): every simulation gets its own noise, and a replay gets the same noise as its copy. Changes process_campaign.py from G2 on; C8's record stays as it is."
- **Statement:** what the card put to the user `[CODE ANN@04977e0]`: `process_campaign.py:219` seeds an iteration's additive recording noise with `noise_seed_base + 1000 * topo_idx + iter_idx` (base 70000), with nothing from the task or the simulation, so equal (topology, iteration) indices give the same noise realization in every task -- in C8 and in August's Giulia run -- and, within one task, iteration 1000 + k of topology t repeats the noise of iteration k of topology t + 1; the ANN sweep draws one `seed_run` per simulation (`hpc/Phenomenological_finalv1/HPC_main_sweep.py:1692`) and states that no two simulations share a noise stream (`:1050-1059`). As built `[CODE ANN sandbox 78ca3c0]`: the scheme `sim`, the new default, seeds `np.random.default_rng` with `SeedSequence([noise_seed_base, key, topo_idx, iter_idx])`, where key is the file's `seed_run`, or 2**32 + crc32('campaign/sweep') when `seed_run` is missing or negative (the sweep's paired mode writes -1); the scheme `topo_iter` is the old formula, bit for bit; every `mea_iter_*.npz` records its `noise_entropy`, and its `meta_json` the scheme. The plan records the tools' scheme and the gate refuses a manifest or a file seeded otherwise; a plan without the key (C8's) skips that check. In C8 no topology holds more than 960 iteration files (0 of the 95 tasks above 1000) `[CLUSTER 2026-10-06, the user's paste of 16:28]`, so C8 has the cross-task sharing and not the within-task repeat.
- **Why:** the option's rationale, not stated further by the user: one noise realization per simulation, as the simulator intends for its own streams.
- **Implies:** `smoke_test_mea_noise.py`, 7 checks `[SANDBOX 2026-10-06]`: `topo_iter` reproduces the C8-state code bit for bit; two tasks with the same files and different `seed_run` get the same noise under `topo_iter` and different noise under `sim`; a replay (the same files, `seed_run` included) gets identical outputs under `sim`; `seed_run` -1 falls back to the task key; `noise_entropy` in every file; iteration 1000 of topology 0 and iteration 0 of topology 1 share their noise under `topo_iter` only. The G2 suite's Q1 (the plan says `sim`) and Q6 (a task re-detected with `topo_iter` fails the Giulia gate). G2's detections are not noise-for-noise comparable with August's `mea_out_1electrode`, which D-066 deletes after G2's record.
- **Does not decide:** whether C8's detections are ever re-detected under `sim` (the option keeps C8's record as it is); another `noise_seed_base`.
- **Sources:** `[user]` the C8 chat, 2026-10-06, the question card; `[CODE ANN@04977e0]`, `[CODE ANN sandbox 78ca3c0]` as cited; `[CLUSTER 2026-10-06]` the topology check; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-06]`.
- **Status:** active; built in the sandbox, not delivered; reaches davinci with D-071's change, after the C8 record. [2026-10-07 `[GITHUB]`: on ANN `main` as `d0ced88`, with D-071; not yet on davinci.] [2026-10-07 `[CLUSTER]`: on davinci since G2a; `smoke_test_mea_noise.py` 7/7 there (G2b); the Giulia plan records `noise seeds : sim` (G2d).] [2026-10-07 15:46 `[CLUSTER]`, G2t: the test task's first `mea_iter` file records `noise_seed_scheme` `sim` in its `meta_json` and carries `noise_entropy`, as the plan's geometry says.]

### D-074 -- The Giulia simulated bank takes all 69 record tasks, with `O_N` declared in the wider box [0.01, 3.0]
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-08 / `[user]` (this chat, a question card answered at about 16:30 Europe/Rome; the user chose "All 69 under the wider box" over the option Claude recommended, "49 tasks, one O_N box (Recommended)").
- **Binds:** B5 (`Sbi-extractor/export_windows.py`, not built; `claude/PLAN_2026-10-08_giulia_B5.md`) and the sim bank of G4 (`~/banks/giulia_sim/`); every sim shard's contract (`bounds_theta`, inside the digest `concat_shards` compares); the prior that B6, G5 and G6 evaluate.
- **Decision (the option's words):** "All 69 under the wider box" -- "Every row kept (September's choice); the O_N prior becomes a known mixture, so posteriors and prior-based diagnostics need a correction."
- **Statement:** every task of the Giulia record (69: v1 25, v2 24, v5 20) is exported, both sides of the 18 twin pairs included. Every shard declares `O_N` in [0.01, 3.0] -- in its `ln` coordinate [ln 0.01, ln 3] = [-4.60517, 1.09861], as September's export did (`EXTRACTOR_USAGE.md` 12.3, 13.1) -- so all shards carry one contract. The 20 tasks whose manifests carry [0.03, 3.0] (v1's 18 twins and 2 v1 cfd tasks `[reasoning, from G2d's x20 / x49 count and D5's per-pair lines]`) drew `O_N` log-uniformly in that narrower box, so in the bank the `O_N` prior is the mixture p_train(ln O_N) = w_a U[ln 0.01, ln 3] + w_b U[ln 0.03, ln 3], w_a and w_b the shares of kept rows from the two groups (after D-075's floor). Each shard records the box its task drew from, outside the digest, so the weights can be computed from the bank.
- **Why:** the user's choice; the option's rationale: every row kept.
- **Implies:** (1) the amortized posterior is learned under p_train; reporting a posterior under the declared box, or using the box as the prior in a diagnostic (the runner's prior floor, SBC or coverage on prior draws, the replicate statistic's analytic Sigma_0), needs the mixture -- an importance weight U / p_train on the `O_N` column, known in closed form, or the mixture itself in those formulas `[reasoning]`; (2) the twins stay, and D-076 keeps each pair's two sides together in the split; (3) about a third of the rows come from the narrower box `[reasoning, from the per-task sizes]`.
- **Does not decide:** how B6, G5 and G6 apply the correction; which 20 tasks exactly (B5's plan step reads every manifest).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the card, 2026-10-08; D-045, D-070; `[CLUSTER 2026-10-07, 2026-10-08]` G2d, N1; `[KB]` `EXTRACTOR_USAGE.md` 12.3, 13.1; `[CODE SBI@9fcbc88]` `hpc/joint/stage1/latent_bank.py:42-60, 184-204`.
- **Status:** active. [corrected 2026-10-08, about 16:55 (the reply of that time): the Statement's "w_a and w_b the shares of kept rows from the two groups (after D-075's floor)" is wrong. NPE fitted on pairs whose theta was drawn from a proposal p~(theta) instead of the prior p(theta) targets the proposal posterior, p~(theta | x) proportional to p(theta | x) p~(theta) / p(theta), for an expressive enough estimator and many simulations `[KB: Greenberg et al. 2019 (APT), sec. 2.3, eq. (1); Deistler et al. 2022 (TSNPE), sec. 2]`. Here p~ is the mixture the labels were drawn from, so its weights are the shares of simulated rows -- the record's iterations in the [0.01, 3.0] and the [0.03, 3.0] tasks -- before the floor, which selects on x alone and does not enter p~(theta | x); the floor only tilts the bank's theta-marginal, p~(theta) P(S | theta) / P(S), a different object.] [2026-10-08 17:10: Implies (1), the correction, is superseded by D-078: the declared box is the prior everywhere and no mixture is computed. The Statement's one declared box, and so one contract, stands.]

### D-075 -- An activity floor on the Giulia simulated windows: mean rate at least 0.1 Hz
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-08 / `[user]` (the same card; "Floor at 0.1 Hz window mean", chosen over Claude's recommendation, "Measure first, then decide (Recommended)").
- **Binds:** B5's row filter; the rows of the Giulia sim bank; D-074's weights.
- **Decision (the option's words):** "Floor at 0.1 Hz window mean" -- "The real arm's electrode threshold (D-044) applied to each window; selecting on x alone leaves p(theta|x) unchanged for kept windows."
- **Statement:** a simulation's row enters the bank only if its window -- the one-electrode IFR x over [0, 180) s at 100 Hz (D-041, D-043, D-044) -- has a time-averaged rate of at least 0.1 Hz, mean_t x(t) >= 0.1 Hz, about 18 detected spikes in 180 s `[reasoning: the smoothed IFR integrates to the spike count, up to the kernel's edge loss]`. B5 counts and records the rows each task loses.
- **Why:** the option's rationale; 0.1 Hz is the value of the real arm's `mfr_threshold` (D-044).
- **Implies:** (1) the bank's pairs are drawn from p(theta, x | S), S = {mean x >= 0.1 Hz}: for every x in S, p(theta | x, S) = p(theta | x) `[textbook, from memory: selection on x alone]`, while the bank's theta-marginal is p(theta | S), proportional to p(theta) P(S | theta) -- so a prior-based diagnostic draws from the bank, not from the box; (2) distinct from D-005, which is unchanged; (3) a real window below 0.1 Hz lies outside the training support of x; B5's real mode counts them.
- **Does not decide:** any floor on the real arm (its selection is per electrode over the whole recording, D-043, D-044).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the card, 2026-10-08; D-005, D-041, D-043, D-044.
- **Status:** active. [2026-10-12 `[CLUSTER 2026-10-12, B1]`: B5.1's estimate of the kept share (D-080): 0.5002, 95% CI 0.4793-0.5215 (one simulation per topology, 3,783 topologies, 2,612 donors; unweighted 0.4985); the window rates' quantiles in Hz: min 0, q05 0, q25 0.00556, q50 0.0944, q75 3.28, q95 19.4, max 79.9 -- the median simulation sits just under the floor; about 630,404 of 1,260,207 rows kept. Implies (1) holds with P(S | theta) far from constant: the bank's theta-marginal is not the box.]

### D-078 -- The Giulia sim bank's `O_N` prior is the declared box [0.01, 3.0]; no mixture correction
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-08 / `[user]` (this chat, 17:10 Europe/Rome), answering the correction in the reply of about 16:55 (the weights of D-074's mixture).
- **Binds:** the prior that B6 (the runner's campaign prior and its prior floor), G5 (calibration) and G6 (posteriors, the replicate statistic's prior covariance, diagnostics) use for the Giulia sim bank; D-074's Implies (1).
- **Decision (the user's words):** "Do not bother yourself with this. Take just the wider box (this is an isolated case however usually the campaigns are run over equal priors)".
- **Statement:** wherever the prior of the Giulia sim bank is used, `O_N` is log-uniform on the declared box [0.01, 3.0] -- the unit interval in the bank's coordinates -- for all 69 tasks, as if the 20 tasks whose manifests carry [0.03, 3.0] had drawn from it; no mixture is computed and no row is reweighted. D-074's one declared box, and so one contract, stands.
- **Why:** the user's: an isolated case; campaigns are normally run over equal priors.
- **Implies:** `[reasoning]` the network targets the posterior under the mixture the labels were drawn from (D-074's Status) and is read as the posterior under the box. The difference sits on the `O_N` axis alone: below 0.03 the mixture's density is nu_a / (nu_a + nu_b ln 300 / ln 100) of its density above -- about 0.62 if nu_b, the share of the record's simulations in the [0.03, 3.0] tasks, is a third -- so where the data do not constrain `O_N`, posteriors under-weight `O_N` < 0.03 by up to that factor, and where they do, the effect vanishes. Calibration checked on the bank's own pairs is consistent with what the network learned; checked on fresh draws from the box, it would show the mismatch on `O_N` alone. Each shard records the box its task drew from (the B5 plan, provenance `[proposed]`), so a correction can be added later without re-exporting.
- **Does not decide:** a later campaign set with unequal priors.
- **Sources:** `[user]` this chat, 2026-10-08 17:10; D-074; `[KB]` Greenberg et al. 2019 (APT) sec. 2.3; Deistler et al. 2022 (TSNPE) sec. 2.
- **Status:** active. [2026-10-15: the runner's campaign prior built (the pipeline build's block 4, `run_joint_arms.py`): a bank whose sidecar's `latent_spec.kind` is `campaign` uses the unit box, L0 = 0 nats, delta_hat = -L; the bench path unchanged.]

### D-080 -- B5.1 also estimates the share of rows the floor keeps and the bank's bytes per row, and prints the free space, as estimates only
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-09 / `[user]` (the same message), answering the second question; the answer Claude recommended.
- **Binds:** B5.1's outputs (its printout and `bank_plan.json`); the disk question of the B5 plan's sec. 4 (about 90.7 GB before the floor and compression; the quota TO VERIFY), before B5.2.
- **Decision (the question's words, answered "yes"):** "Should B5.1 also estimate the share of rows S keeps and the compressed bytes per row?
