### D-082 -- The search and the best fit run from a configuration file: rounds, GP and training budgets, a wall budget with resume across jobs, seeds
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-13 / `[user]` (the second card, in the user's own words, spelling kept): "We will do n rounds in which the search over the hyperparameters of the joint architecture is carried out with the GP optimization. All these optimization and training parameters must be set in advance thus just make this possible. Moreover if the search doesn't conclude in the wall time (300h) it must resume till the search is complete or till a wall time set by me (let's say 450) is reached. After that the best fit across seeds will be done with much training batches (also in this case the configuration params must be set by me, I need therefor a configuration file)."
- **Binds:** the driver's search and best-fit stages (the work plan's S7, S8; block 7); SBI `stage4/npe_tune_joint.py` and `jobs/launch_joint_tune.sh` as the search's tools; `stage3/run_joint_arms.py` and the job scripts (a checkpoint per epoch and `--resume`, block 6); the configuration file `hpc/pipeline/configs/<tag>.json` `[proposed]`.
- **Decision:** the search runs n rounds of GP optimization over the joint architecture's hyper-parameters; every optimization and training parameter is set in advance in a configuration file (rounds, trials per round, the GP's initial points and seed, each trial's training budget and resources); if the search has not concluded within one job's wall time it resumes, until it is complete or a total wall budget the user sets (450 h in the example) is reached; then the best configuration is fitted across seeds with a larger training budget ("much training batches"), its parameters also from the configuration file.
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** the "300h" is davinci's `cpu` queue cap per job `[KB, the C8 handoff v1.10: "the cpu queue allows 300:00:00"]`, so the resume is across jobs: a trial killed at its walltime is resubmitted and continues from its last epoch's checkpoint, while the sum of the trial jobs' used wall time (`qstat -xf`, `resources_used.walltime`) stays under `max_total_walltime_h`; a round is complete when every trial of it has a ledger entry; the search is complete after `rounds` rounds or at the budget, and the best fit then takes the lowest held-out NLL in the ledger. The configuration's draft is the work plan's sec. 2, its values `[proposed]` for Giulia and the user's to set.
- **Why:** the user's: the parameters are the user's to set, in advance; a search that does not fit one job must not be lost.
- **Implies:** blocks 6 (checkpoint and resume; the jobs' resources from the config) and 7 (the search and best-fit stages) of the work plan; the trial budget against about 630,000 kept rows is to be set before the first search (the committed job sees 32,000 rows `[KB stage plan G5]`).
- **Does not decide:** the values (rounds, budgets, seeds, resources) -- the user's, in the file; the GP's space (`npe_tune_joint.py space`; D-038's open parts unchanged).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the second card, 2026-10-13; `[KB]` the C8 handoff v1.10 (the queue cap), the usage guide v1.11 secs. 5, 7; `[CODE SBI@9fcbc88]` `stage4/npe_tune_joint.py:376-432`, `hpc/npe_tune_search.py:247-290`, `stage2/joint_train.py:124-271`.
- **Status:** active; the configuration file to write with block 1. [2026-10-14: written as config v1 with the values of D-085; it reads `max_total_walltime_h` as the elapsed time since the search started, queue waits included -- the likelier reading of the user's sentence -- not as the summed trial time of the Statement above.]

### D-084 -- The runner reads the bank through a streaming shard loader; no row dropped
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-13 / `[user]` (the second card; "Streaming loader (Recommended)": "I change the runner to read shards one at a time and sample batches from them (an SBI code change, tested on the bench banks first); no row is dropped").
- **Binds:** SBI `stage1/latent_bank.py`, `stage2/joint_batches.py`, `stage2/joint_train.py`, `stage3/run_joint_arms.py` (the work plan's block 5); G5's sizing (the kept rows are about 630,404 x 18,000 float32 = 45.4 GB in memory `[reasoning]`, against `concat_shards`, which loads the whole bank).
- **Decision:** the runner reads the bank's shards through a streaming loader -- the small arrays of every shard once, the windows on demand from a rolling buffer of shards -- so that a node's memory bounds the buffer, not the bank; no row is dropped and no cap per task is introduced.
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** the simulated stream draws its batches from a buffer of `loader.buffer_shards` shards (one task's simulations per shard, sorted by topology), replacing one shard at a time, with one torch generator per stream as now (J9); the grouped split by donor is computed from the small arrays alone; the validation and report evaluations run shard by shard; the diagnostics' posterior samples keep today's cap of 256 report rows `[proposed]`. Tested on the bench banks first: the same split hash and report-split NLL as the in-memory runner when the buffer holds every shard.
- **Why:** the user chose it over refusing (the size reported, the decision per data set) and over a cap on rows per task (which changes which simulations train the model).
- **Implies:** block 5 of the work plan before any Giulia training element (G5).
- **Does not decide:** `buffer_shards` (proposed 4, about 2.9 GB of windows); whether the within-buffer sampling changes training against i.i.d. sampling from the whole bank (to measure on the bench, block 5).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the second card, 2026-10-13; `[CODE SBI@9fcbc88]` `stage3/run_joint_arms.py:274-281, 338-341, 366-369`, `stage2/joint_batches.py:106-175`, `stage2/joint_train.py:104-122`; `[KB]` the B5 plan v1.7 sec. 6 (the kept rows).
- **Status:** active; to build. [2026-10-15: built -- block 5 of the pipeline build (`sci/pipeline` `3bd94ec`): `latent_bank.ShardIndex` (the small arrays once, the row count from the .npy header, the windows on demand), `joint_batches.ShardStream` (the rolling buffer with a QUOTA rule: every row one expected draw per pass whatever the shards' sizes -- a first schedule, advance every n_oldest / B steps, was replaced before testing because its draws per row depended on the neighbouring shards' sizes), the runner's `--buffer-shards K`; K >= the shard count reproduces the in-memory runner bit for bit; the selection and report splits evaluated with at most two shards resident; the within-buffer correlation left to the bench as decided (the test fixture: L 2.9094 at K = 99 against 3.1878 at K = 1 after 3 epochs, a number, not a verdict). `buffer_shards` 4 stays config v4's value.]

### D-085 -- The configuration's values: an expressive NPE network, a large GP search and a large training budget, set by Claude (config v1)
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-14 / `[user, delegated]` (the B5 chat, 16:11: "I honestly have to re read all the parameters. I would like you to set them to have expressive NPE network and also a large amount of budget for the GP search (both initial and the actual search) and the training as well.").
- **Binds:** the Giulia configuration file, `hpc/pipeline/configs/giulia.json` v1 (sent to the user 2026-10-14; committed with block 1); the SBI code its values need (`claude/PLAN_2026-10-13_giulia_pipeline.md` v1.2, sec. 3a); the five choices left open on 2026-10-13 (that plan, sec. 4a).
- **Decision:** the user sets the direction and leaves the values to Claude: an expressive NPE network, a large GP budget for both the random initial design and the guided search, and a large training budget. The user inspects the file before any submission (2026-10-13 15:24).
- **Statement (Claude's values, config v1):** campaign S-A1, arm A1 (D-039). The space: `depth_exponent` [3, 5] (F-s narrowed), `embedding_size` [16, 64], `hidden_features` [64, 512] (the width rule at `embedding_dim` 64), the other axes at the code's ranges; conditioners of 2 hidden layers whatever `num_transforms` (F-ac); `num_bins` 10 reaching the runner (F-a, D-038). The GP: 48 random configurations as round 0, then 6 rounds of 24 guided ones (N = 192), at most 24 trials at once; a proposal seed (0) apart from one training seed (0) for every trial, so one donor split. A trial: 6 passes over the training split in 12 epochs (steps from the batch size), 24 cores, 160 GB, 120 h, resubmitted with resume at most twice; a budget gate on the space's costliest configuration before round 0. The wall budget: 600 h elapsed. The best fit: seeds 1-5 (fresh splits; five members, as the SBI practical guide's ensemble), 40 passes in 40 epochs, early stop after 10 epochs without improvement, 512 posterior draws, 48 cores, 200 GB, 300 h.
- **Why:** the user's direction; the values rest on the code read and the checks of 2026-10-13/14 `[SANDBOX]` (that plan, secs. 4a, 4b). On a synthetic 12-axis objective with a known optimum, through the repository's `propose()`: the GP's advantage over random search grew with N (none reliable at 24 trials, clear at 48); at N = 192 the schedule of config v1 (48 random, then 6 x 24) ended at a regret of 0.011-0.017 on 4 of 4 seeds, against 0.15-0.43 for 192 random configurations and 0.035-0.057 for the same schedule stopped at N = 96, while 96 random then 4 x 24 was not reliably different (lower on 2 of 4 seeds) -- a toy: the direction carries, not the sizes. The costliest configuration of the narrowed space (ResNet blocks, depth 5, width 3, fusion) trains a row in about 0.55 s on one sandbox core, the depth-6 ResNeXt corner in 1.8 s (both with sbi's default 12-layer conditioners).
- **Implies:** the five choices of 2026-10-13 taken as Claude's -- the wall budget as elapsed time, a separate proposal seed, a budget in passes, F-s narrowed, N = 192; the SBI changes of that plan's sec. 3a before the search can run as configured (blocks 6 and 7); D-082's Statement on the summed wall time superseded on that point (D-082's Status).
- **Does not decide:** that the user accepts these values (the inspection is pending; any value may change); D-038's other open parts; the posteriors command (D-083).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the B5 chat, 2026-10-14 16:11; `[KB]` the pipeline plan v1.1 sec. 4a; `Simulation Based Inference A Practical Guide.pdf` (full text: an ensemble of five NPEs for calibration; a 31-parameter neural simulator fitted with 10 spline transforms of two hidden layers); `[CODE SBI@9fcbc88]`; `[CODE sbi 0.27.0]` `neural_nets/net_builders/flow.py:1142-1143`; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-14]` the checks in `config_v1_checks_20261014`.
- **Status:** active; config v1 delivered 2026-10-14, the user's inspection pending. [2026-10-15: amended by D-086 -- `head_pool_ops` is searched; config v1's `search.space_overrides` gains `"head_pool_ops": [0, 1]` and the search has 13 free axes; the file was sent again.] [2026-10-15: amended by D-088 -- the user set the split (85/10/5), the cores per trial (48), the best fit's seeds (one), `n_initial_points` (50), the GP rounds (20 of 10) and a trial's epochs (20); config v2; Claude's `max_concurrent_trials` 25 and best-fit seed 1 follow from them; the other values stand as Claude's.]

- **Binds:** the Giulia search (S7, campaign S-A1): `giulia.json` (`search.space_overrides`); the joint space (`hpc/joint/stage4/joint_space.py`: `JOINT_KNOB_ORDER`, `JointSpaceSpec`, `space_dimensions`, `default_joint_space().fixed`); the tuner (`stage4/npe_tune_joint.py`: `AXIS_TO_FLAG`); the runner (`stage3/run_joint_arms.py`: a `--head-pool-ops` flag, `make_backbone`); the pipeline plan's sec. 3a.
- **Decision:** `[user]` `head_pool_ops` is searched by the GP instead of held fixed.
- **Statement:** a 13th free axis of the Giulia search, categorical over the two levels the DSN defines, `HEAD_POOL_OPS_LEVELS` (`hpc/dsn/condition_space.py:113`): code 0 = `("mean",)`, code 1 = `("mean", "max", "std")` -- for each encoder stage, the statistics over time of every channel that the head concatenates (all stages under `head_fusion` 1, the last one under 0) before its Linear layer to E outputs. The configuration records the code; the runner decodes it with `decode_head_pool_ops` (`:263-268`) into `BackboneConfig.head_pool_ops`. For this search it replaces the space's fixed code 1 (`default_joint_space().fixed`). Today no flag carries that code to the runner, so every trial builds code 0, the `BackboneConfig` default (F-b); D-038's fix alone would have made every trial build code 1.
- **Why:** the user's call. The cost: at the costliest configuration of config v1's space (ResNet blocks, depth 5, width 3.0, fusion, E 64; flow hidden 512, 12 transforms, 2-layer conditioners), code 1 triples the head's input (640 to 1,920 features) and adds 84,480 parameters to 20.07 M, 0.6 % CPU time per training row (0.558 against 0.554 s) and 48 MB of step memory at batch 8 `[SANDBOX 2026-10-15, check_head_pool_cost.py; one thread, synthetic inputs]`.
- **Implies:** (i) code, in the pipeline plan's blocks: the runner's `--head-pool-ops CODE` and `make_backbone` forwarding the decoded tuple (block 6); the axis in the joint space, free in S-A1 and mapped in `AXIS_TO_FLAG` (block 7). A point of S-A1 then has 13 coordinates (the GP's transformed space 15), the space 24 axes, and a trial id changes with them; a ledger written before the change lacks the axis, and `load_observations` drops such records, so it cannot seed this search. (ii) config v1: `search.space_overrides` gains `"head_pool_ops": [0, 1]`, and the budget gate's costliest configuration takes code 1. (iii) D-038's `head_pool_ops` fix, in the Giulia search, passes the configuration's code rather than `spec.fixed`'s.
- **Does not decide:** whether the other campaigns (S-A2, S-A5, S-A25) and the DUP15HD searches search it too (`[proposed]`: they pin it at code 1, the value the joint plan's S5.1 fixes, `hpc/joint/JOINT_DSN_NPE_PLAN_v0_6.md`, once the axis is in the space); whether the Stage 3 runner's own default moves from code 0 to 1 (D-038's open part (i)); whether `n_initial_points` grows with the 13th axis (Claude keeps 48, about 3.7 random configurations per free axis, within D-085).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the B5 chat, 2026-10-15 11:35 and 11:44; `[CODE SBI@9fcbc88]` `hpc/dsn/condition_space.py:113, 263-268`, `hpc/dsn/backbone.py:47, 76, 339-393`, `hpc/joint/stage3/run_joint_arms.py:282-331` (`make_backbone` sets no `head_pool_ops`; under `hpc/joint` the name appears only in `joint_space.py`'s docstring and `fixed` dict, `:41, 323`, and in a docs inventory tool), `hpc/joint/stage4/joint_space.py:262-336`, `hpc/joint/stage4/npe_tune_joint.py:266-296` (`load_observations` drops a record missing any axis of `JOINT_KNOB_ORDER`, `:293`); `[KB]` D-038 (F-b), `claude/joint_docs/P0_PARAMETERS_OVERVIEW.md` S3.2 note 2; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-15]` `check_head_pool_cost.py`.
- **Status:** active; to build (the pipeline plan's blocks 6 and 7). [2026-10-15: its open `n_initial_points` settled by D-088: 50, about 3.8 per free axis.] [2026-10-15: the runner's `--head-pool-ops` built (block 6, `3bd94ec`): code 1 triples the head's in-features in the test; the axis in the tuner is block 7.] [2026-10-16: built in full -- block 7 (`sci/pipeline` `fd07397`): `head_pool_ops` is an axis of the joint space (`joint_space.JOINT_KNOB_ORDER`, appended to the encoder block), pinned at 1 by every campaign and freed by the configuration's override `[0, 1]` (`apply_overrides`); the search stage passes the override on every tuner call; 13 free axes for Giulia.]

### D-087 -- The joint loop's early-stopping margin stays at 1e-6 nats per row
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-15 / `[user]` (the B5 chat, 12:44: "let' s keep it inert"), answering Claude's report of 12:27 that the value has no recorded reason and is practically inert.
- **Binds:** `hpc/joint/stage2/joint_train.py:229` (`rec["val_npe"] < best - 1e-6`); block 6's `--patience` (the pipeline plan, sec. 3a); config v2's `best_fit.patience_epochs`.
- **Decision:** `[user]` the margin stays as the code has it; no calibrated improvement threshold for the NLL.
- **Statement:** an epoch e improves iff L_sel(e) < min over e' < e of L_sel(e') - 1e-6, with L_sel(e) the mean of -log q_omega(theta | h_psi(x)) over the selection split's rows at the end of epoch e, in nats per row; a non-improving epoch adds one to the count e - e_best, an improving one resets it to 0; the loop stops at the first e with e - e_best >= P and restores e_best's weights (`joint_train.py:150, 227-232, 262-270`). Unlike the DSN's silhouette threshold (2 sigma of a label-shuffle null, measured once and frozen; `hpc/dsn/silhouette_floor.py`, `train.py:775-830`), it is not derived from data.
- **Why:** the user's call. What the margin does: it differs from sbi's strict `<` (`sbi 0.27.0 inference/trainers/base.py:1243`) only on gains of at most 1e-6 nats per row; on 5,000 synthetic loss curves per (E, P) in {(40, 10), (12, 99), (100, 40)} it changed the restored epoch on 2, 0 and 1 curves, each time between two epochs within 1e-6 nats `[SANDBOX 2026-10-15, check_patience_rules.py]`. No reason for the value is recorded: no comment at `:229`; the commit that introduced it, `581fa98` (2026-09-04, the first implementation of Stages 1-3c), does not mention it; `hpc/joint/docs/P5_OPTIMISER_AND_SCHEDULE.md` lists it as a code constant (F-i, report).
- **Implies:** block 6's `--patience` keeps the margin; nothing to build for it. The history keeps L_sel per epoch (`val_npe`), from which a calibrated margin could be computed later.
- **Does not decide:** whether search trials get a patience (config v2 sets none: all 20 epochs run); the best fit's P (config v2: 10, Claude's within D-085).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the B5 chat, 2026-10-15 12:18-12:44; `[CODE SBI@9fcbc88]` `hpc/joint/stage2/joint_train.py:150, 227-232, 262-270`, `hpc/dsn/train.py:99-131, 452-474, 775-830, 860-929`, `hpc/dsn/silhouette_floor.py`, git `581fa98`; `[CODE sbi 0.27.0]` `inference/trainers/base.py:1225-1253`; `[REPO]` `hpc/joint/docs/P5_OPTIMISER_AND_SCHEDULE.md` (the optimiser rows; F-i); `[SANDBOX 2026-10-15]` `check_patience_rules.py`.
- **Status:** active. [2026-10-15: `--patience` built (block 6) with the margin as it is; the counter survives a stop and resume.] [2026-10-16: the stages pass `--patience 99` to the trials and `best_fit.patience_epochs` (10) to the members (block 7, `fd07397`).] [corrected 2026-10-20: the code compares L_sel(e) with L_sel(e_best), the last improving epoch's value, not with the minimum over e' < e (`joint_train.py:204, 315-316`); the two differ only on dips of less than 1e-6 below L_sel(e_best) -- a curve creeping down 9e-7 per epoch: e_best 19 by the code, 3 by the Statement `[SANDBOX 2026-10-20, test_smoke_perrow.py, mutation rule]`.]

### D-088 -- The user's values for the Giulia search and best fit: split 85/10/5, 48 cores per trial, one seed, 50 initial points, 20 GP rounds of 10, 20 epochs per trial

(ii) Code: the fractions need a runner flag and the tuner passing it (blocks 6-7); they change the split hash and every trial id. (iii) Consequences `[SANDBOX 2026-10-15, check_config_v2.py; estimates: per-row costs from one sandbox core, an assumed 10-24x speed-up on 48 cores]`: 22 waves (2 for round 0, then one per GP round); a costliest-size trial takes about 20-49 h, so the 600 h elapsed cap (D-085) ends the search before round 20 if the mean wave, queue waits included, exceeds about 27 h; the best fit of a costliest-size winner about 5-13 days without an early stop; the report split, on which the GP's objective is computed, has 131 donors (392 in v1). (iv) No ensemble: the joint stack has no ensemble code (`hpc/joint` searched); the standalone NPE stack has one (`hpc/npe_tune_train.py:346-350`: sbi's `EnsemblePosterior`, the uniform mixture of the members' densities, `hpc/npe_model.py:30-40`).

### D-089 -- The finalist is fitted as an ensemble of 10 members, combined as the uniform mixture
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-15 / `[user]` (the B5 chat, 12:56, quoting Claude's formula for the plan's ensemble: "Okok perfect use 10 instead for the finalist.").
- **Binds:** S8 of the pipeline plan (the best fit) and its block 7; `giulia.json` config v3 (`best_fit.split_seed`, `best_fit.member_seeds`); the runner's seed handling (`hpc/joint/stage3/run_joint_arms.py`: `--seed` drives the split, the torch seed and the stream generators, `:369`, P0 Table B); the posteriors command (D-083), which will read the members.
- **Decision:** `[user]` M_ens = 10 for the finalist, instead of the joint plan's 5.
- **Statement:** the finalist -- the search's configuration of lowest report-split NLL -- is trained ten times, as independent (encoder, flow) pairs (psi_m, omega_m), m = 1..10, each with the best-fit budget, and the ten are combined as the uniform mixture q_bar(theta | x) = (1/10) sum over m of q_omega_m(theta | h_psi_m(x)), theta in the unit box (0, 1)^23, x in R^18000 -- the arithmetic mixture of sbi's `EnsemblePosterior` with equal weights (`sbi 0.27.0 inference/posteriors/ensemble_posterior.py`: weights 1/M by default; `log_prob` = logsumexp of log w_m + log q_m). Claude's reading of D-088's "for now one seed only" next to it, to confirm with the user: the members share one donor split (split seed 1) and differ in their training seeds (initialisation, batch order), `member_seeds` 1-10.
- **Why:** the user's call, after Claude reported that the joint plan designs the ensemble (Stage 4, M_ens = 5, D7: independent pairs) and that the joint driver never built it (F-ap: no subcommand; `n_members = 1` in `npe_tune_joint.py:330-336`).
- **Implies:** (i) code, block 6: a split seed apart from the training seed (`--split-seed`, default `--seed`, so a call without it behaves as today); block 7: S8 submits ten member jobs and records each; the ensemble's report-split NLL, -(1/n) sum over rows i of [logsumexp over m of (-nll_m,i) - log 10], computed from the members' per-row NLL files (`run_joint_arms.py:537, 628-633`: `_perrow.npz` holds `nll` per report row), which requires the shared split, written to `BEST_FIT_COMPLETE.json` beside each member's NLL. (ii) Cost `[SANDBOX 2026-10-15, check_config_v3.py; estimate: per-row costs from one sandbox core, an assumed 10-24x speed-up on 48 cores]`: a costliest-size member 126-303 h without an early stop; ten in parallel on ten nodes, 61-145 k core-hours in all. (iii) The identity behind (i): log M - logsumexp_m(-nll_m) equals the direct -log((1/M) sum_m exp(-nll_m)) to 1.8e-15 nats on 10 x 1000 random rows (float64), and the ensemble's row NLL never exceeds the members' mean (Jensen) `[SANDBOX 2026-10-15, check_config_v3.py]`.
- **Does not decide:** the shared split (Claude's reading; the alternative, one split per member as in the standalone NPE stack, `hpc/npe_model.py:228-236`, leaves no common held-out rows for the ensemble's NLL); the ensemble's calibration diagnostics; the posteriors command (D-083); ensembles for other finalists or a top-k.
- **Sources:** `[user]` the B5 chat, 2026-10-15 12:44-12:56; `[CODE SBI@9fcbc88]` `hpc/joint/stage3/run_joint_arms.py:369, 537, 622-634`, `hpc/joint/stage4/npe_tune_joint.py:330-336`, `hpc/joint/JOINT_DSN_NPE_PLAN_v0_6.md` (D7, Stage 4), `hpc/joint/docs/P6_SEARCH_DRIVER.md` (F-ap), `hpc/npe_tune_train.py:346-350`; `[CODE sbi 0.27.0]` `inference/posteriors/ensemble_posterior.py`; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-15]` `check_config_v3.py`.
- **Status:** active; to build (block 7); the shared split to confirm with the user. [2026-11-12 10:28 (the LindDUP chat): the shared split reaffirmed for every ensemble the pipeline fits -- the members share `best_fit.split_seed` and differ only in `best_fit.member_seeds` (initialisation and batch order), synth's best fits (M = 5, split seed 1, seeds 1-5) and block 18's fits of the other arm included -- in the user's words "keep it that way, same split seed but different training seeds". Asked after Claude's answer that the user's reading (different split seeds per member, the test kept common) is not the standard deep ensemble: Hermans et al. 2022 define it as members trained independently on the same dataset and report that bagging ensembles (members on bootstrap resamples) do not always raise coverage `[KB, full text]`. Not taken: a common test set with the training and early-stopping donors re-split per member. The code is unchanged: it already does this (`synth.json` `best_fit`, `hpc/pipeline/stages/best_fit.py:119-126`) `[CODE SBI@462a05a]`.] [2026-10-15, about 15:40: the shared split confirmed by the user in the build chat (the build handoff's Q1): `split_seed` 1 for every member, `member_seeds` 1-10; the ensemble's report NLL is computed on the shared report rows.] [2026-10-15: `--split-seed` built (block 6): two members at seeds 1 and 2 on split seed 1 share the report rows' `group` array and differ in weights; the stem is `<arm>_split<s>_seed<k>` when the split seed differs from the seed; the member jobs and the ensemble NLL are block 7.] [2026-10-16: built -- block 7 (`fd07397`): `stages/best_fit.py` submits one job per member seed with `--split-seed 1`, resubmits a killed member with `--resume`, and `tools/ensemble_nll.py` computes L_bar = -(1/n) sum_i [logsumexp_m(-l_{m,i}) - log M] from the members' per-row files (the donors and theta of every member checked equal); the smoke test: M copies of one member give that member's L within 1e-12, two members give L_bar <= their mean (Jensen); `BEST_FIT_COMPLETE.json` is the artefact.]

### D-090 -- The search's wall budget is 400 h elapsed; whether to resume past it is decided afterwards
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-15 / `[user]` (the pipeline build chat, about 15:45 Europe/Rome, answering the build handoff's Q4: "Lets put it at 400h and see afterwards if resume").
- **Binds:** `search.max_total_walltime_h` of the Giulia configuration (config v4, `hpc/pipeline/projects/giulia/giulia.json`); the search stage of the pipeline (the plan's block 7): the elapsed clock and `SEARCH_COMPLETE.json`.
- **Decision:** `[user]` the cap is 400 h; the resume question is deferred to when it binds.
- **Statement:** `max_total_walltime_h` = 400, read as config v1 reads it (D-085; `walltime_reading` "elapsed"): the time since the search's first submission, queue waits included. At the limit no new round is proposed; the trials already submitted run to their end and enter the ledger; `SEARCH_COMPLETE.json` records what ended the search -- round 20 evaluated, or the cap -- with the rounds done and the elapsed hours. A later extension (more rounds from the same ledger under a new cap) is possible by design, since the GP is refitted from the ledger before every round and nothing but the clock is lost; whether to extend is the user's call at that time.
- **Why:** the user's. [estimate: at 22 waves and a costliest-size trial of about 20-49 h on 48 cores, a 400 h cap binds when the mean wave, queue waits included, exceeds about 18 h (600 h: 27 h); the budget gate measures the real time before round 0.]
- **Implies:** config v3's 600 becomes 400 in config v4 (`config_version` 4, the only value changed); block 7 makes the extension a re-run of the same command under a raised cap -- the state file keeps the clock's origin and the rounds done, and a configuration whose cap exceeds the elapsed hours of a search that the cap ended re-opens it `[proposed]`.
- **Does not decide:** whether the search is extended; the cap of a later data set.
- **Sources:** `[user]` the build chat, 2026-10-15; D-085, D-088; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-15]` `check_config_v3.py` (C4: the cap binds at a mean wave of 27.3 h at 600 h; 400 / 22 = 18.2 h).

Resuming past the cap: raise the value and `--retry search` (`--accept-config-change`); the ledger keeps every trial.] [corrected 2026-10-20: that resume was never built -- at `04cb7ab` `SEARCH_COMPLETE.json` is the search's artefact of record (`stages/__init__.py` `artefact_of`, kind `file`): a `--retry search` after a budget end is skipped at its first tick, and the best fit starts as soon as the search ends (`pipeline.py:770-808`). ### D-091 -- The pipeline code is data-set-agnostic; every project value lives in the project's folder in the repository, SBI `hpc/pipeline/projects/<tag>/`
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-15 / `[user]` (the build chat, about 15:45, answering Q5: "The whole pipeline should be agnositc from the specific project. There are however specific features in each project (the paths the manifests and so on) that must live in the project's folder"; then the follow-up card: "In the repo: SBI hpc/pipeline/projects/giulia/").
- **Binds:** block 1's layout and every later block: SBI `hpc/pipeline/` (the stage sequencer `pipeline.py`, `stages/`, `run_pipeline.sh`, `README.md`, the tests) holds no project value; `hpc/pipeline/projects/<tag>/` holds the project's -- for Giulia `giulia.json` (config v4) and, later, whatever project artefact the configuration refers to by digest. The branch `sci/pipeline` (Claude's name).
- **Decision:** `[user]` the pipeline is agnostic of the project; the project's specifics (paths, manifests, ...) live in the project's folder, in the repository.
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** the code may read a project value only through the configuration object loaded from `projects/<tag>/<tag>.json`; a project name, path, glob, digest or resource written in `hpc/pipeline/*.py`, `stages/*.py` or `run_pipeline.sh` is a defect. The run state (`state.json`, logs, the written bank profile) goes to the configuration's `work_root` on davinci, not to the repository. A new data set is a new folder `projects/<tag>/` and the same command. The project-specific files that other tools read today -- the cohort config under `hpc/dsn/hpc/Config/`, the freeze and the frozen manifest under Sbi-extractor `artifacts/`, the bank profile under `export_windows/profiles/` -- stay where those tools read them; the project folder points to them.
- **Why:** the user's: one command per data set (D-081) holds only if nothing of the data set is in the code.
- **Implies:** the handoff's Q5 ("where the driver lives") is settled as SBI `hpc/pipeline/` plus the project folder; `specs/SPEC.md` on `sci/pipeline` states the no-project-value rule as a testable constraint (the code grepped for the project tag and its paths); the configuration schema is the project folder's contract.
- **Does not decide:** whether the cohort config and the freeze move into the project folder later; the posteriors command (D-083).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the build chat, 2026-10-15; D-081, D-082; the build handoff sec. 6 (Q5).
- **Status:** active; to build (block 1). [2026-10-15: built (block 1, `3549e60`): the folder `hpc/pipeline/projects/giulia/`, the grep in the smoke test.] [2026-10-16: holds through block 7 (`fd07397`): the stages take every path, queue and value from the configuration; the block-7 test greps again.]

### D-094 -- The simulated bank heals itself: a verification job after every round; bad tasks quarantined and rerun
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-18 / `[user]` (the build chat, about 12:05: the card's first question, "What should the pipeline do so a sick node can't stop or corrupt a run? Pick all that apply.", answered "Self-healing bank (Recommended)", after the user's message of 11:59, "This should not happen. Always submit jobs", which breaks off there).
- **Binds:** S5 of the one-command pipeline (`hpc/pipeline/stages/bank.py`, `SimBankStage`) for every data set; its artefact of record; config v6 (`bank.verify`, `bank.max_rerun_rounds`).
- **Decision (the option chosen, its words):** "After the array, a separate verification JOB re-reads every shard and checks every zip member's CRC. Bad or missing tasks are moved to a quarantine folder and rerun (at most 2 rounds), then the gate runs."
- **Statement (Claude's reading, as built in SBI `sci/pipeline` `f1a2b7a`):** every round is the array and its gate, then the verification job `tools/bank_verify.py` (stdlib; through `jobs/run_argv.pbs` in `envs.extract` on `bank.queue`, 8 cores, 16gb, 02:00:00 `[proposed]`): each task's marker, each shard's and sidecar's sha256 against the marker, every zip member read to its end, the leftovers, the record. Bad or unfinished tasks are quarantined (D-095) and the array runs again with round 0's walltime, at most 2 reruns, then STOP naming the tasks; a global problem (a shard of no chunk, a marker of another plan) stops the run with nothing moved; most tasks unreadable by I/O errors alone mean a suspect verifier: verify again. The stage is done when `BANK_VERIFIED.json` + .sha256 certifies `BANK_RECORD.json`. The gate still runs every round -- "then the gate runs" read as: the gate stays, and the certificate needs both.
- **Why:** the 2026-10-16 run: two tasks that finished on `dvnode012` (exit 0, markers written) left shards that are not zip files `[CLUSTER 2026-10-18]`; a marker's sha256 is taken after the write on the writing node, and the gate reads only the small arrays (D-006's Status).
- **Implies:** the davinci run resumes with `--retry sim_bank --accept-config-change` (config v6); every later data set's bank is verified the same way.
- **Does not decide:** where the bank lives (the home file system or another); keeping jobs off a node (the admins'); the real bank (one shard written on the login node, not checked by this job).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the build chat, 2026-10-18 11:59-12:05; `[CLUSTER 2026-10-18]` the check of 11:59; SBI `specs/SPEC.md` block 2.
- **Status:** active; built 2026-10-18 (`f1a2b7a`; the block-2 test 11 pass, 1 skip; blocks 1, 3, 7, 8 pass; five mutations caught). [2026-10-18 12:48 `[CLUSTER, the user's paste of 14:02]`: the first run on davinci -- round 0's verification job on `dvnode001` read 155 shard files and 1705 zip members in 13.7 s, `VERIFY OK`, the certificate written; nothing quarantined, no rerun round.]

### D-095 -- A bad bank task is quarantined by moving, never deleted, without asking
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-18 / `[user]` (the same card, its second question, "When the pipeline finds a bad task (corrupt shard, or no marker), may it move that task's marker and shards into a quarantine folder by itself? Nothing is ever deleted.", answered "Yes, move automatically (Recommended)").

- **Statement (as built):** `os.rename` within `paths.banks_root` (a move across file systems is refused, never copied); the folder `<banks_root>/<tag>_sim_quarantine/<YYYYmmdd_HHMMSS>_round<r>/` keeps the files under their names relative to the bank folder, with `QUARANTINE.json` (each move's from, to, bytes, task, reasons; the verification report and its sha256); the record and the certificate move with the tasks, since the bank they described changes.
- **Why:** D-081 (never pausing); the evidence of a fault stays on disk for a later look.

### D-096 -- A trial that dies of a node fault is resubmitted, its record set aside
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-18 / `[user]` (the build chat: the card's first question, "When a trial dies of a node fault (SIGBUS, or an I/O error such as Errno 5/107/108/116 in its log), what should the pipeline do? (Today such a trial is recorded 'failed', which is final: never rerun, ignored by the GP.)", answered "Resubmit it (Recommended)", after the read-only check pasted at 14:28).
- **Binds:** S7 of the one-command pipeline (`hpc/pipeline/stages/search.py`) for every data set; config v7 (`search.resubmit_node_faults`). Applied by Claude to S8's members (`best_fit.resubmit_node_faults`) `[proposed]`: the question named the trials only.
- **Decision (the option chosen, its words):** "Record moved aside (kept), rerun with --resume up to 2 times, then 'killed'; today's 3 rejoin round 0."
- **Statement (Claude's reading, as built in SBI `sci/pipeline` `1b2b36f`):** a trial whose ledger record says `failed` is `examining` (not terminal) until its evidence is read -- the record's error, and its last job log once that log has its `RUN_ARGV EXIT:` line or 300 s have passed. A node fault is the runner killed by SIGBUS (`runner exited -7`; `RUN_ARGV EXIT: 135` or `249`; `Bus error`) or a file-system error in the log (`[Errno 5|107|108|116|121]`: EIO, ENOTCONN, ESHUTDOWN, ESTALE, EREMOTEIO, or their texts, or `Could not chdir to home directory`). Then the record is moved to `<id>.json.nodefault<k>` (kept), the pending spec rewritten, and the trial queued again with `--resume` within `trial.max_resubmits` -- the counter the walltime kills use -- then the pipeline's `killed` record (revivable by `--retry search`). Any other failure stays `failed`, final (SIGKILL, a full disk, a missing file and an exception without those errors included). A trial recorded failed before this code is examined at the next poll. A member whose log shows a node fault is resubmitted like a kill instead of failing the stage.
- **Why:** the read-only check of 2026-10-18 14:28 `[CLUSTER]`: trials `16641a4d79ce`, `4006d8ff495f`, `42f269b8ecb8` died on `dvnode012` of its file system, not of their configurations; a failed record was final, so three of round 0's 50 design points were lost.
- **Implies:** the davinci restart (the timed stop of the running sequencer, step 1, the dry run and the run with `--accept-config-change`); the three trials rejoin round 0 when the throttle has slots.
- **Does not decide:** keeping jobs off a node (the admins'; an email to them drafted 2026-10-18); what a failure of the configuration itself means for the GP (still dropped, not imputed).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the card, 2026-10-18; `[CLUSTER 2026-10-18 14:28]` the check; SBI `specs/SPEC.md` block 7 (amended 2026-10-18).
- **Status:** active; built 2026-10-18 (`1b2b36f`; `test_smoke_block7b.py` 6 pass, 1 skip; four mutations caught; blocks 1, 2, 3, 7, 8 pass; the old-to-new restart rehearsed on the stand-in). [2026-10-19 `[CLUSTER, the user's paste of 15:48]`: the first driver job (1852795) raised `JSONDecodeError` on a trial's ledger record that existed and held no JSON -- what a write by rename without fsync leaves when the node goes down `[reasoning]`; the login-node sequencer most likely died on it at 05:52Z (rehearsed with `f1a2b7a` itself: it raises and leaves no lock, as seen at 10:28). The rule extended `[proposed]`, SBI `sci/pipeline` `b2f5833`: such a record is read again for 300 s, then set aside as `<id>.json.unreadable<k>` and the trial taken as a job that ended without a record (`--resume` within `max_resubmits`; no pending spec left -- an ok record whose bytes were lost -- recorded killed); the members alike; the pipeline's JSON and the tuner's records fsynced; `tools/search_report.py` (read-only) shows what the next poll does with each trial. `test_smoke_block7b.py` 6 pass, mutation `unreadable` caught.] [2026-10-19 17:08 `[CLUSTER, the user's paste of the report, 95b42ae, --qstat]`: EVERY failed record of round 0 is a node fault on `dvnode012` -- 18 records (15 SIGBUS `runner exited -7` after `BrokenPipeError [Errno 108]` on the home mount, 3 `OSError [Errno 5]` reading bank shards) plus the empty record (`ed55f0f26286`, dvnode012, exit 249, 7 min) -- each job dead 7-19 min after its start, 18 Oct 12:54 to 19 Oct 05:42: the node frees first, so the next queued trial lands there (a black-hole node); 19 of the 50 round-0 trials lost to it, 23 ok, 8 still running. The failed configurations span the ok ones' ranges (the knob table): not the configurations, not memory (max 76.5 GB of 160). The next tick queues the 19 again, each with 2 resubmissions: while `dvnode012` takes jobs they may go there again -- keeping jobs off a node is the admins' (this entry's "does not decide"); the user's call before the submission.] [2026-10-29: amended by D-111 (`66e18ef`) -- beside the node faults, an environment fault: a job that exits 96 (no environment on its node; 97 / 98 from a job script before D-111) or that PBS could not start (`Exit_status` -1 to -20) is submitted again at once, not a resubmission, and counts as a node fault of its host for D-102; 12 in a row on 3 or more hosts stop the stage.]

### D-097 -- A checkpoint whose zip CRCs fail is moved aside, and the trial restarts from epoch 0
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-18 / `[user]` (the same card, its second question, "A sick node can also write a corrupt checkpoint (as it wrote corrupt bank shards on 16 Oct). Before resuming a trial, may the pipeline check its checkpoint's zip CRCs and, if bad, move it aside and restart that trial from epoch 0?", answered "Yes, move it aside (Recommended)").
- **Binds:** S7's trials; applied by Claude to S8's members `[proposed]`. The rule "Claude never deletes data; the user does, through a list-then-delete tool with a CODE" stands: a move is not a deletion.
- **Decision (the option's words):** "Kept as <name>.corrupt<n>, never deleted (as D-095); the trial restarts clean."
- **Statement (as built, `1b2b36f`):** before every submission of a trial or a member, each `*_train_ckpt.pt` of its run folder is opened as a zip archive and every member's CRC-32 checked (`zipfile.ZipFile.testzip`); a file that is not a readable archive, or a member that fails, is moved to `<name>.corrupt<k>` and logged, and the job starts from epoch 0; a file the login node cannot read (an OSError) is left in place and logged.
- **Why:** torch.save writes the checkpoint as a zip archive with a CRC-32 per member; one flipped byte passes `torch.load` without an error and returns altered weights, a truncated file does not load `[SANDBOX 2026-10-18, torch 2.14.1]`; on 2026-10-16 the same node wrote bank shards that passed every check but a full read.
- **Does not decide:** when the `.corrupt` files are deleted (the user's call, through the list-then-delete tool).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the card, 2026-10-18; SBI `specs/SPEC.md` block 7.
- **Status:** active; built 2026-10-18 (`1b2b36f`).

### D-098 -- Nothing may depend on a particular login node: the sequencer runs as PBS jobs on compute nodes
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-18 / `[user]` (the build chat, 16:33, on the timed stop of the old sequencer on `dvlogin04` given at 15:44: "Avoid this kind of approach in the future because now I cannot anymore go to that specific login node. so write down that this approach must not be used. The point is that I always log in from a different node so do not use this approach / Do not worry about the former node. just rewrite everything to not use this approach only submissions to compute nodes as usual").
- **Binds:** every pipeline, tool and line Claude gives the user for davinci, in every chat of this project and for every data set; SBI `hpc/pipeline/` (SPEC block 1, amended 2026-10-18 (c); config v8). Supersedes D-081's "one background process on the login node (`nohup`)".
- **Decision (the user's words):** "only submissions to compute nodes as usual"; an approach that needs a specific login node "must not be used".
- **Statement (Claude's reading, as built in SBI `sci/pipeline` `3d96804`):** no step may depend on a particular login node: nothing left running on a login node (no `nohup`, no `&`), no `ps` or `kill` there, no step that must return to the node it started on. The sequencer is a chain of PBS jobs `pl_<tag>` (the configuration's `driver` block; Giulia `cpu`, 4 cores, 16gb, 24:00:00, a tick every 60 s `[proposed]`): each submits its successor at once (`-W depend=afterany`), ticks until its walltime less min(max(900 s, 5 x poll), walltime / 4) and exits; a killed one is followed by its successor, which takes the dead job's lock; a job already queued or running under the same name and output file is adopted, not submitted twice; three unclean ends in a row end the chain. The user's commands (the submission, the dry run, `--status`, `--stop`, `--retire-login-sequencer`) run on any login node and only submit, read and write files on the shared file system.
- **Why:** the old sequencer runs under `nohup` on `dvlogin04` since 12:55 and the user's logins no longer reach that node, so the stop given at 15:44 (a timed `kill` there) cannot be run.
- **Implies:** the one-time transition `--retire-login-sequencer`: a directory `state.json.tmp` makes the old code's next state save fail, so it exits through its `finally` and removes its lock -- rehearsed on the code of `f1a2b7a` itself, alive and already dead `[SANDBOX 2026-10-18]`; the probe `tools/probe_compute_qsub.pbs` (qsub, qstat, qselect, qdel from a compute node) before the submission; the davinci lines of the README of `hpc/pipeline/`.
- **Does not decide:** a fallback if a compute node cannot submit jobs (the probe's answer is pending); the driver block's values (Claude's); whether the `hpc-git-delivery` skill carries the rule too.
- **Sources:** `[user]` the build chat, 2026-10-18 16:33; SBI `specs/SPEC.md` block 1 (amendment (c)) and sec. 4.
- **Status:** active; built 2026-10-18 (`3d96804`; `test_smoke_block1b.py` 6 pass, 1 skip, nine mutations caught; blocks 1, 2, 3, 7, 7b, 8 pass). Not yet run on davinci. ### D-099 -- The whole pipeline holds at most `resources.max_cores` cores at once: 200 for now, raisable
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-19 / `[user]` (the build chat, 11:50: "You will have available 200 cores so put this cap (that can be risen) for the whole pipelinefrom the extraction to the joint DSN adn NPE optimization and training").
- **Binds:** every stage of SBI `hpc/pipeline/` for every data set -- S1 the extraction, S2 the re-detection, S3 the label freeze, S4-S6 the banks, S7 the search (the joint DSN+NPE optimisation), S8 the best fit (the training) -- and the sequencer's own job (D-098); config v9 (`resources.max_cores`).
- **Decision (the user's words):** 200 cores available; "this cap (that can be risen) for the whole pipeline".
- **Statement (Claude's reading `[proposed]`):** at every moment the cores requested by the pipeline's jobs that are queued, held or running, the sequencer's own job included, sum to at most `resources.max_cores`: a job is submitted only when it fits, else it waits; an array counts the elements that can run at once (its `%` limit, else all of them); a job that waits on another (a gate, an aggregation) counts once that one has ended; nothing already submitted is deleted for the cap. Raising it: the value in the project's configuration, then `--stop` and a submission with `--accept-config-change`. The per-job sizes stand (D-088: 48 cores per trial; D-071: a re-detection task uses its node's 48), so at 200 with the sequencer's 4: 4 trials, 4 members, 4 re-detection tasks at once.
- **Why:** the user's allocation.
- **Implies:** code in blocks 1 (the count, in the sequencer), 2, 3 and 7 (each stage submits within it); a launcher with no limit of its own (S1's array: 2 cores per well, `-J` without `%`) is launched only when all of it fits, and refused when it alone exceeds the cap; the 13 trials submitted before the cap (48 cores each) finish on their own, and no new trial starts until the pipeline is under it; at 200 cores the sequencer's job never waits for a slot behind the pipeline's own trials (the 12-job queue cap seen on 2026-10-19).
- **Does not decide:** the per-job sizes (D-088, D-071 stand); memory (not capped); the user's jobs outside the pipeline (not counted); the search's wall budget (D-090), which at 4 trials at once may end the search before its 20th round `[estimate]`.
- **Sources:** `[user]` the build chat, 2026-10-19 11:50; `[CODE Sbi-extractor@e9aeb55]` the launchers' requests (S1 `extractor/run_extractor_array_mea.pbs:51`, `launch_stage_d.sh:152`; S2 `sim_reextract/launch_sim_reextract.sh:97-98, 169-170` and its gate's header, 8 cores; the bank `export_windows/launch_bank.sh:64-68, 194-195`).
- **Status:** active; built 2026-10-19 (SBI `sci/pipeline` `e449fb5`, config v9; tests `72ff9fa`, `f33feb6`): `test_smoke_block1c.py` 7 pass, 1 skip, five mutations caught; `test_cores` in blocks 2, 3 and 7, seven one-off mutations caught; blocks 1, 1b, 1c, 2, 3, 7, 7b, 8 pass `[SANDBOX 2026-10-19]`; on davinci `test_smoke_block1c.py` 7 pass `[CLUSTER 2026-10-19 14:51]`. As built, two points sharpen the Statement `[proposed]`: (1) a job recorded after another (a gate after its array, the aggregation after the extraction array) is counted WITH it as the larger of the two cores, never both, so the gate's cores are kept while its array runs (the Statement's "counts once that one has ended" would let the array's room be given away before the gate starts); (2) a job that can never fit (more than `max_cores` less `driver.ncpus`) stops its stage with the reason instead of waiting for ever (S2 and the bank check their gate at their start). Found on the way and fixed: the bank launcher would have read the sequencer job's `NCPUS` (4) for its tasks (D-098 put the stage inside a PBS job); the stage passes the launcher's own defaults. The run in flight: the 13 trials submitted before the cap keep running; no new job starts until at most 3 remain; the 12-running-jobs limit no longer binds the pipeline (4 trials + the sequencer's job), so the slot of v1.15's reading is not needed. `[estimate]` at 4 trials at once the 400 h clock (D-090) ends the search before round 20 unless a trial averages under about 5 h. [2026-10-30: Giulia's value now 300 (D-115; 400 from 2026-10-19, D-101); LindDUP's 200 (D-109).]

### D-102 -- A node that kills three trials of a campaign is avoided: a job that lands there holds its slot and exits, and the stage resubmits it at once
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-21 / `[user]` (the build chat, 16:29, on the two open items of the report of 16:26 -- `dvnode012` and the knowledge base: "Avoid this for the next timesss"; the question card's answer "Build it as proposed").
- **Binds:** SBI `hpc/pipeline/`: the search stage (block 7) and the best-fit stage (block 8), their job script `jobs/run_argv.pbs`, D-096's node-fault count; a new file in the work root (`avoid_nodes.json` `[proposed name]`); SPEC block 7 (an amendment). Supersedes D-100's "the pipeline does nothing about `dvnode012`".
- **Decision (the user's words):** "Avoid this for the next timesss"; the card: build it as proposed.
- **Statement (as proposed `[proposed]`, not built):** for each campaign, a host on which the pipeline has counted 3 or more node faults (D-096's "N node fault(s) on <host> so far") is written to an avoid list in the work root. A job of the search or of the best fit reads the list when it starts, before it activates its environment: if its host is on the list it writes one line to its log, sleeps 10 minutes holding the slot (so the scheduler does not refill that node with the next job at once), and exits with a dedicated code. The stage reads that code and resubmits the trial at once, not counted as a resubmission (`max_resubmits` and the pending spec untouched). The user can read the list and clear it: the file is plain JSON; removing a host, or the file, lifts the ban, and the count that re-adds it starts again from the faults after the clear `[proposed]`. The threshold (3) and the hold (10 min) are constants with an environment override, not configuration keys, so the config digest does not change and the running chain needs no `--accept-config-change`.
- **Why:** `dvnode012` has killed 13 trial attempts and 2 trials (`killed` after `max_resubmits`) since 2026-10-18 (D-096's Status; the paste of 2026-10-21 15:30 `[CLUSTER]`); the admins have not taken it out; PBS Pro gives a user no way to exclude a node in `select` (the C8 handoff v1.16 `[KB]`, now `docs/kb_archive/claude/HANDOFF_2026-10-01_c8_push_and_run.md`).
- **Implies:** a trial that lands on a banned node loses 10 min and a queue wait instead of hours of training and one of its resubmissions; the slot cost is a 48-core node idle for 10 min per landing; the list is per work root, so per data set; D-096's healing stays as it is for the first two faults of a node.
- **Does not decide:** the threshold beyond 3 and the hold beyond 10 min (Claude's, overridable); whether a ban expires on its own (no: until cleared `[proposed]`); whether S1, S2 and the bank stages read the list (not now: their tasks are short and D-095 quarantines their outputs); the admins' handling of the node.
- **Sources:** `[user]` 16:29 and the card; `[CLUSTER 2026-10-21 15:30]` the 13 faults; `[KB]` the C8 handoff v1.16 (OpenPBS forum: no user-side node exclusion); D-096.
- **Status:** active; BUILT 2026-10-21 as proposed, SBI `sci/pipeline` `435a78f` (SPEC block 7, amended 2026-10-21): `avoid_nodes.txt` in the work root (one host per line, comments above; appended by `common.note_node_fault` at the third fault of a host in a stage's run; a line deleted by hand is seen at the next poll and the host counted again from then); `jobs/run_argv.pbs` holds `AVOID_HOLD_S` (600 s) on a listed host and exits 94; the search and the best fit submit such a trial or member again at once, `resubmits` unchanged; the dry runs and `tools/search_report.py` say so; `host_of_log` reads the AVOID line. Tests `[SANDBOX 2026-10-21]`: `test_smoke_block7b.py` 6 pass, 1 skip (`avoid_units`, `avoid_list` end to end on the fault fixture with `FAKE_PBS_HOSTS`); the mutations `avoid_add`, `avoid_hold`, `avoid_sync` each caught; blocks 1, 1b, 1c, 2, 3, 7, 8, report, reset, perrow pass; the clean clone passes the README's step 1. Not yet run on davinci: it loads at the chain's next hand-over after the pull (no configuration key, so no `--accept-config-change`), or at once with `--stop --wait 180` and a submission; `dvnode012` is listed only after its third fault under the new code (the count starts with the new driver's state: the 13 earlier faults are in the old state's `node_fault_hosts`, which the running state keeps -- so if the state's count for it is already 3 or more, the next heal lists it at once `[reasoning from the code: note_node_fault reads sub['node_fault_hosts'] as it is]`). [2026-10-21 17:52 `[CLUSTER, the user's paste of 17:53]`: LOADED -- step 1 `PIPELINE CODE OK` at `435a78f`; `--stop --wait 180`: the held successor `1875207` deleted, `1864297` stopped at its tick, `stop: the sequencer has stopped` (17:52:18), its 8 trial jobs running on; the dry run exit 0, `search: would run`, both `avoid list (D-102): /davinci-1/home/ldellamea/pipeline/giulia/avoid_nodes.txt; ... holds its slot 600 s ...` lines, `in flight per the state file: 384 in 8 job(s)`; `SUBMITTED the sequencer: job 1877075.pbsserver01 (pl_giulia)` at 17:52:41 (log `driver/driver.8.log`). `avoid_nodes.txt` created by hand with the header and `dvnode012` (comment `2026-10-21T15:53:03Z by hand: 13 node faults by 2026-10-21 15:30`). The 8 trials then running were submitted by the old code without `AVOID_FILE`: one of them on `dvnode012` is not held until it ends. `1877075`'s `started` line not yet seen (a read-only line sent).] [2026-10-22 `[CLUSTER, the user's paste of 11:40]`: WORKING -- `driver 1877075.pbsserver01 on dvnode002: started; its successor 1877076.pbsserver01 waits for it (afterany)` at 2026-10-21 17:52:55; at 11:39 the state 30 s old, the lock `job:1877075.pbsserver01 on dvnode002`, `1877075 R, 1877076 H`. ### D-103 -- When the knowledge base runs short, the superseded handoffs and plans are archived in the repository and deleted from it, without asking
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-21 / `[user]` (the build chat, 16:29; the question card's answer "Adopt the rule").
- **Binds:** this project's knowledge base (the 2,000,000-byte cap) and the SBI repository, `docs/kb_archive/` on `sci/pipeline`; complements D-020 (document upkeep) and D-093 (KB writes limited to the log, plan rows and existing handoffs). Narrows the project instructions' "ask me first for deleting a doc" to this one case.
- **Decision (the user's words):** the card, "Adopt the rule".
- **Statement:** when `project_info` shows fewer than 20,000 bytes free, Claude moves into the repository, under `docs/kb_archive/<original path>` and byte for byte, the handoffs and plans that a later handoff or plan names as replaced, commits and pushes them, then deletes them from the knowledge base; every move is listed in the reply and in `docs/kb_archive/README.md` with what supersedes it. Never: a file the user uploaded, a current document, this log, the path maps, the usage guides, the deck pack. A document no later one names as replaced is asked about, not moved.
- **Why:** the knowledge base was at about 1,600 bytes free on 2026-10-21 (the build handoff v1.37), and a refused write loses a decision (D-093's Why); the archive keeps every superseded document retrievable by its path and commit.
- **Implies:** applied the same turn -- eight documents, 663,016 bytes, SBI `6daa5df`: `claude/HANDOFF_2026-09-19_migration.md`, `claude/HANDOFF_2026-09-21_stageC.md`, `claude/HANDOFF_2026-10-01_giulia.md`, `claude/PLAN_2026-10-01_giulia_hpc_stages.md`, `claude/HANDOFF_2026-10-01_c8_push_and_run.md`, `claude/HANDOFF_2026-10-02_joint_docs_P3_done.md`, `claude/PLAN_2026-10-06_giulia_G2.md`, `claude/JOINT_DOCS_BUILD_PLAN_v1.md`; `project_info` after: 1,743,445 of 2,000,000 bytes. A reference to one of them in a current document now resolves to `docs/kb_archive/<path>` in the repository. The copies were taken through the session transcript, not retyped (pure ASCII, LF, the byte counts in the README).
- **Does not decide:** restructuring this log (the Open-calls row of 2026-10-09; D-020); `HANDOFF_hpc_implementation_v1_1.md` at the top level (its provenance is not on record: asked before any move); whether the archive is ever pruned (no, as read).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the card; `project_info` 2026-10-21 before (the 1,600 figure, v1.37) and after; SBI `6daa5df` `[SANDBOX 2026-10-21]`.
- **Status:** active; applied 2026-10-21.

### D-105 -- LindDUP is run by configuration: no code change specific to it
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-29 / `[user]` (14:36).
- **Binds:** how LindDUP reaches the cluster: its project folder (D-091) and its Sbi-extractor cohort profile, not code written for it.
- **Decision (the user's words, spelling corrected):** "The code should be flexible enough to not need any change for this specific subproject. Let's just revise the configuration parameters".
- **Statement:** the pipeline and the tools it drives are used as they are; LindDUP's values live in `projects/linddup/linddup.json` and `sim_reextract/profiles/linddup.sh`. Where the code cannot take LindDUP, Claude says so before building on it.
- **Why:** D-081's aim: a new data set in one command, without weeks of work.

### D-107 -- The contact-gate probability `stoa_gate_p` enters theta as a topology axis
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-29 / `[user]` (the question card, about 14:57: "In: inferred, p = 34").
- **Binds:** the LindDUP label: the freeze (S3), the bank plan and the bank (S4-S6), `bank.topology_axes` and `bank.expected_p` of `linddup.json`, the search's shape anchors; Sbi-extractor `preflight_label_axes.py`, `sbi_labels.py`, `export_windows/bank_plan_model.py`, `bank_plan_load.py`, `bank_plan_sample.py`, `bank_row.py`, `bank_export.py` and their tests.
- **Decision:** `stoa_gate_p` is inferred.
- **Statement (Claude's reading, the code to build `[proposed]`):** theta = the campaign's active run_args (its manifest's `active_indices`: 32 under `tripartite`) followed by the topology block [`conn_prob`, `stoa_gate_p`], the block linear in natural units (the existing rule for topology axes); the prior box of `stoa_gate_p` is the campaign's own [`stoa_gate_lo`, `stoa_gate_hi`] from each task's `job_args.json` (the launcher's default [0.2, 1.0]), as `conn_prob`'s comes from `conn_prob_lo` / `conn_prob_hi`; p = 34 if the campaign used `tripartite` -- read on the data before the run. The value of each row is read from the raw iteration files (`process_campaign.py` does not copy it): once per topology, its first and last iteration compared, since the sweep draws it in the outer topology loop `[CODE ANN@d0ced88, smoke_test_stoa_gate.py T3, T5]`; the plan's sampled simulation per topology checks it a third time. The contract signature of `sim_reextract_plan.py` is not changed (the records of C8 and G2 keep theirs); the bank's own rule that every task's label bounds agree (`label_columns`) covers the gate's box. No ANN change (the card's option named one; Claude's design avoids it `[reasoning]`). A campaign without the axis, or where it is constant, is unchanged: the freeze reports it ABSENT or CONSTANT, as for any topology axis.
- **Why:** the user's; the gate is a swept, consumed axis of the MEAMA design (`smoke_test_stoa_gate.py` T3).
- **Implies:** one Sbi-extractor commit with D-108 and the LindDUP profile, through the laptop (D-062), its suites rerun; Giulia's bank, plan and record unaffected (their labels have no gate).
- **Does not decide:** a gate in other campaign families; a gate on a log coordinate (its box spans less than a decade).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the card; `[CODE Sbi-extractor@e9aeb55]`, `[CODE ANN@d0ced88]` as cited.
- **Status:** active; to build. [2026-10-29: built as stated, not run on davinci -- one Sbi-extractor commit on `e9aeb55` with D-108 and the LindDUP profile, tree `413e707a`, delivered as a patch for the laptop push (D-062). `sbi_labels.py`: the gate in the topology block (`KNOWN_TOPOLOGY_AXES`, `JOB_ARGS_TOPOLOGY_BOUNDS`, `topology_bounds_from_job_args`, `build_label_spec(..., topology_bounds=...)`; the legacy four axes keep their own bounds and refuse a box from `job_args.json`); `preflight_label_axes.py` takes its known axes from `sbi_labels`; the bank plan reads the box from each task's `job_args.json` and the value from the raw iteration files (first and last per topology compared, the sampled iteration a third time; a missing, non-scalar or non-finite value refuses); the export compares each task's label box with the plan's. ### D-111 -- Once the pipeline is started, an environment fault never ends it: a node without conda costs a delay, never the chain, a resubmission or a trial's progress
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-29 / `[user]` (the build chat, 14:59, on the Open call "A compute node without the conda installation ends the chain and eats trials": "2) The node-without-conda fix: Fix this it must be bullet proof as teh whole pipeline once starte MUST BE").
- **Binds:** SBI `hpc/pipeline/` for every data set: the job script `jobs/run_argv.pbs`, `jobs/activate_env.sh` and `jobs/chain.sh` (new), `envs.run_in_env`, the search and best-fit stages, the sequencer and its chain (`pipeline.py`); the runner's checkpoint (`hpc/joint/stage2/joint_train.py`). SPEC: block 1 amendment (g), block 6 amendment (B6c), block 7 "Environment faults and reruns" (a)-(f), sec. 8 (D-111's residuals). Amends D-096 (a class of fault beside the node faults) and D-098 (the chain also heals from the jobs' side); D-102's avoid list counts the new class.
- **Decision (the user's words):** "Fix this it must be bullet proof as teh whole pipeline once starte MUST BE" (read: fix it; it must be bulletproof, as the whole pipeline must be once it is started).
- **Statement (Claude's design, built in SBI `sci/pipeline` `66e18ef`; the constants Claude's, `[proposed]`):** once submitted, the pipeline is not ended by an environment fault, and such a fault costs no resubmission and no trial's progress. (a) The activation (`pipeline_activate`): conda as before (`set +u` across the hook and the activation); when conda is missing on the node or fails there, the environment's own folder -- the prefix the submission recorded (`<work_root>/envs/<env>.prefix`), then `$HOME/.conda/envs/<env>`, then each `CONDA_ENVS_PATH` folder -- the first whose python starts on the node, activated as conda activates one environment (its activate.d scripts included) and logged `RUN_ARGV ENV: host <h>: conda unavailable (...); <env> activated from its folder <p>`; when nothing works, `RUN_ARGV ENV FAULT: ...` and exit 96. The sequencer's own commands in an environment take the same path. (b) An environment fault -- a trial or member job that exits 96 (or 97 / 98 from a job script before D-111), or one PBS could not start or keep on its node (`Exit_status` -1 to -20, PBS Pro's JOB_EXEC codes) -- is submitted again at once: not a resubmission, the pending spec untouched; a job that exited 96 first holds its slot 600 s (as D-102's hold). It counts as a node fault of its host, so a host is listed at its third (D-102). A guard: 12 environment faults in a row (no job of the stage running to its end in between) on at least 3 hosts stop the stage, with a message naming the hosts and what to do -- the environment or the cluster, not a node. (c) Every trial and member job runs with `--resume` from its first submission; the runner fsyncs its per-epoch checkpoint, and a checkpoint it cannot load is moved aside (`<file>.corrupt<k>`) and the run starts from epoch 0 -- so a job PBS runs again continues from its last epoch. (d) A sequencer job that cannot start its command (an environment fault, its work directory or argv file missing) submits a replacement of itself, unless another job of the chain is queued, held or running, to start 300 s later, the delay doubling with each replacement since the last full tick, up to 3600 s. (e) Every trial or member job's end, whatever its outcome and a hold included, checks the chain: when no `pl_<tag>` job is queued, held or running and the chain has not ended on purpose (`<work_root>/driver/CHAIN_END`), it submits a sequencer job from the chain's template (`driver/chain.qsub`, `driver/revive.argv`, written at every driver submission). (f) The ends on purpose, which write `CHAIN_END`: the pipeline done; a stage failed (the guard of (b) among them); a refused start (a configuration change without `--accept-config-change`); `--stop`; three sequencer jobs in a row ended uncleanly (D-098). A submission removes `CHAIN_END`. The constants are not configuration keys (environment overrides: `PIPELINE_ENV_FAULT_STREAK` 12, `PIPELINE_ENV_FAULT_HOSTS` 3, `AVOID_HOLD_S` 600, `PIPELINE_REPLACE_BASE_S` 300, `PIPELINE_REPLACE_MAX_S` 3600, `PIPELINE_WATCHDOG_STALE_S` 600), so the configuration's digest is unchanged and loading needs no `--accept-config-change`.
- **Why:** the read-only check the user pasted at 14:59 `[CLUSTER]` (D-098's Status, corrected 2026-10-29): PBS ran the driver `1904837` and the two trial jobs then running (`1905463`, `1905466`, both with checkpoints) again on 27 Oct at 12:36:58 on dvnode007, dvnode008 and dvnode013, where `/archive/apps/miniconda/miniconda3/py312_2` was not mounted; all three exited 98 within 2 s, and the successor `1906402`, run at 12:37:22 on dvnode007, exited 98 within 3 s: no driver was left, the log said nothing, and the search stood still until the user's check two days later. Had conda been there, the trials' reruns would have started from epoch 0 (their first argv had no `--resume`).
- **Implies:** loading it: the pull and step 1 at `66e18ef`, `test_smoke_block7c.py activate_units job_units` on a login node, `--stop --wait 180`, the dry run (its `environment faults (D-111)` lines) and a submission, which records the two environments' prefixes (`environment <env>: <prefix> (recorded for nodes without conda, D-111)`); no `--accept-config-change`. The folder fallback helps where the environment's folder is reachable: one under the home (`/davinci-1/home`, mounted on the nodes of 27 Oct -- their jobs read the user's `.bashrc`) starts on a node without `/archive`; one under `/archive` cannot, and its faults are then held, counted and submitted again free.
- **Does not decide:** the residuals (SPEC sec. 8): a sequencer job PBS cannot start while no trial or member job runs (between stages, or after a round's last trial) leaves the chain dead until a stage job ends or the user submits again; a cluster on which no job can start stops everything until it recovers (the replacement loop then tries at most once an hour). Two deliberate ends the cluster can still trigger -- the guard of (b) and the unclean limit of D-098 -- and whether they should pause and retry instead of ending (Open calls, 2026-10-29). The admins' handling of the nodes.
- **Sources:** `[user]` 2026-10-29 14:59; `[CLUSTER]` the check pasted at 14:59 (`qstat -x -f` of `1904837`, `1906402`, `1905463`, `1905466`; `pbsnodes dvnode007`; the tail of `driver.13.log`); SBI `specs/SPEC.md` at `66e18ef`; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-29]` the tests below.
- **Status:** active; BUILT 2026-10-29, SBI `sci/pipeline` `66e18ef` (rebased on the LindDUP chat's `6d5c6f2`, no conflict) `[GITHUB 2026-10-29, ls-remote]`. Tests `[SANDBOX 2026-10-29]`: `test_smoke_block7c.py` 6 pass, 1 skip; the mutations `fallback`, `replace`, `watchdog`, `chain_end`, `env_free`, `always_resume`, `streak` each caught; blocks 4-6 10 pass, 1 skip (B6c: an empty, truncated or garbage checkpoint moved aside, the run equal to the run without one bit for bit); every regression suite passes (pipeline, 1b, 1c, 2, 3, 7, 7b, 8, report, reset, perrow; Stage 2 40/40, Stage 3 33/33, Stage 4 8/8 and 9/9); vermin minimum 3.5, pyflakes clean, ASCII and LF, the agnostic grep; a clean clone passes step 1, and `activate_units` and `job_units` pass there (`2 of 2 checks pass in 5 s`). Not tested here: davinci's PBS (the `qselect -s` letters, the `-a` time format, which `Exit_status` codes its server reports, qsub from a node without conda), a real conda hook and activate.d scripts, the real hold and backoff durations. Not yet loaded on davinci: the sequencer `1915296` (submitted 14:58:02) runs the code of before. Announced to the user as D-105 in the chat's message of 15:11, a number the LindDUP chat had taken (v1.92). [2026-10-29 17:14 `[CLUSTER, the user's pastes of 17:04 and 17:32, dvlogin02]`: LOADED -- step 1 at `66e18ef` (`D-111 PRESENT`, `PIPELINE CODE OK`), `activate_units` and `job_units` `2 of 2 checks pass in 5 s` on dvlogin02; `--stop --wait 180` deleted the held `1915297` and stopped `1915296` at 17:02:06 (a second stop at 17:02:37 wrote `CHAIN_END`, which the submission removes); the sequencer `1917793` started at 17:14:38 on dvnode002 (`driver <id> on dvnode002: started; its successor 1917794.pbsserver01 waits for it (afterany)`), submitted from the checkout pulled to `66e18ef`; the two round-4 trials `1915298` (`687a28caa9d5`) and `1915299` (`97ac4b147713`), submitted at 14:58:18 by the code of before (no watchdog at their end), R; no `could not start`, `watchdog` or `NODE FAULT` line. ### D-112 -- LindDUP runs on the simulations available: S2's plan leaves out the tasks still being written
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-29 / `[user]` (the LindDUP chat, 17:37, on caution 1 of the reply of 17:20 and the Open call "LindDUP and a campaign still being written").
- **Binds:** S2 of every data set, through configuration (`sim.plan_args`): Sbi-extractor `sim_reextract/sim_reextract_plan.py` (`--quiet-hours`); SBI `hpc/pipeline/stages/inputs.py` (S2 plans again at each round), `tools/inputs_check.py` (block 9's rows), `projects/linddup/linddup.json` v2 (`sim.plan_args` `--quiet-hours 6`). Amends D-104's Implies (the campaign need not be finished); applies D-060 and D-073 (no task re-detected while it is written).
- **Decision (the user's words, spelling corrected):** "Caution 1: the MEAMA campaign is probably still being written. Yes it is, we will run on the available simulations".
- **Statement (Claude's design, built; the 6 h Claude's, `[proposed]`):** S2's plan leaves out every task whose folder, JSON files or `topo_*/` folders changed less than H hours before the plan (`--quiet-hours H`; default 0, off, so the plans of C8, G2 and Giulia are unchanged) and names it in the plan's `excluded` (`still being written (D-112): changed <age> h before the plan, less than --quiet-hours H`) and `excluded_active`, before the replay check reads its files; a plan in which every task is so young is refused (no runnable task), and so is a young task that already has detections in the output root (D-060, D-073). Every launch of S2 plans again (`launch_sim_reextract.sh` runs the plan in each mode), so a task gone quiet since joins the next round, and the gate's record names the tasks it holds. LindDUP: H = 6. The inputs check (block 9) reads the option from `sim.plan_args` (`plan args`; `plan option`: the Sbi-extractor checkout must take it), names the tasks the plan would leave out now (`being written`; FAIL when every task is), samples only the others, and measures the campaign's pace (`pace`: the 95th percentile and the longest gap between two iteration files of one task, by modification time; WARN when the 95th percentile is not under H, since a task still running could then look finished).
- **Why:** the user's. The MEAMA array `1544754[]` was in state B at 17:02 (v1.93); a task re-detected while it is written gives a record of a moment, not of the task (D-060, D-073).
- **Implies:** LindDUP's bank holds the tasks quiet for 6 h when S2's last launch plans; a task finished after S2's record is outside this bank -- a later pass needs a root and a record of its own, as D-073 did for v12's task0011 `[reasoning]`. 6 h is Claude's window: L3's `pace` row says whether it is short for this campaign (a WARN to raise before L5). The freeze (S3) reads one iteration file per topology of the whole campaign and skips an unreadable one with a WARNING (`preflight_label_axes.py:106-129`), so a task being written does not stop it; the bank plan (S4) takes its tasks from S2's record (`export_windows/bank_plan.py:138`), so a task left out does not enter the bank `[CODE Sbi-extractor@4ef7576]`.
- **Does not decide:** when, or whether, the tasks finished after S2's record are re-detected and banked. A task quiet for more than H that is then written again (an element resumed after a long wait) would be re-detected in part `[reasoning]`; L3's qstat lines show such elements.
- **Sources:** `[user]` 2026-10-29 17:37; `[CLUSTER]` the Giulia build chat's paste of 17:04 (v1.93); `[CODE Sbi-extractor@4ef7576]` as cited; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-29]` the tests in Status.
- **Status:** active; BUILT 2026-10-29, not run on davinci. Sbi-extractor: `sim_reextract_plan.py --quiet-hours` (`exclude_active`, `task_newest`) in commit `4ef7576` (tree `9d8ea296`, on the D-107 commit `8e95f86`), delivered for the laptop push (D-062: `sbix_linddup_d107_to_d113.mbox` from `e9aeb55`, `sbix_d112_d113.patch` from `8e95f86`; the run sheet's L0); `smoke_test_sim_reextract.py` D1 (off by default; 6 h leaves the young task out; every task young: refused; -1: refused; a young task with detections: refused; a young replay twin left out before the replay check) and the mutations M4 (the quiet test inverted) and M5 (the young tasks left out after the replay check), each caught `[SANDBOX 2026-10-29]`. SBI `sci/pipeline` `fa51751` `[GITHUB 2026-10-29, ls-remote]`: `linddup.json` v2, S2's rounds (D-113), block 9's rows; `test_smoke_inputs_check.py` `test_being_written` and the mutations `being written`, `plan option`, `plan args`, I1 (the young tasks sampled) and I2 (the plan's window ignored), each caught `[SANDBOX 2026-10-29]`.
