### D-113 -- Every job the pipeline submits, in any repository and for any data set, starts through its job protocol (D-111 extended)
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-29 / `[user]` (the LindDUP chat, 17:37, on caution 2 of the reply of 17:20 and the Open call "D-111's reach for LindDUP's S2 and S5").
- **Binds:** SBI `hpc/pipeline/`: `jobs/stage_job.sh` (new), `stages/common.py` (`stage_job_protocol`, `free_round`, `counted_round`, `unrun_dependent`), `stages/inputs.py` (S1's and S2's launchers; S2's restarts and rounds), `stages/bank.py` (S5's), `tools/inputs_check.py` (rows `protocol (SBI)`, `protocol (Sbi-extractor)`); SBI SPEC blocks 2 and 3 amended 2026-10-29 (b), block 7 (g), block 9, sec. 8. Sbi-extractor: `env.sh` (`sbix_pipeline_v`), the launchers `extractor/launch_stage_d.sh`, `sim_reextract/launch_sim_reextract.sh`, `export_windows/launch_bank.sh`, the job scripts `extractor/run_extractor_array_mea.pbs`, `run_cohort_manifest.pbs`, `sim_reextract/run_sim_reextract_gate.pbs`, `export_windows/run_bank_task.pbs`, `run_bank_gate.pbs` and the new `sim_reextract/run_mea_task.pbs` (S2's tasks: Astro-Neuron-Network's `submit_mea_array.sh` inside the protocol; ANN unchanged); its SPEC (coverage (b), Block 7.4). Extends D-111; applies D-102 (the avoid list) and D-094 (the bank's rounds).
- **Decision (the user's words, spelling corrected):** "Caution 2: D-111 doesn't cover all of LindDUP: This should be agnostic (independent) from the subproject run. Therefore extend with this in mind".
- **Statement (Claude's reading -- the reply's option (b), both repositories -- and design, built; the constants Claude's, `[proposed]`):** D-111's protection belongs to the pipeline, not to a data set. (a) Every job a stage submits through another repository's launcher gets the job protocol's variables in its `qsub -v` -- `PIPELINE_JOBS_DIR` (this checkout's `hpc/pipeline/jobs`), `WORK_ROOT`, `CHAIN_NAME`, `AVOID_FILE`, `AVOID_HOLD_S`; a value with a comma or a blank is refused -- and its job script starts through `jobs/stage_job.sh`: `pipeline_stage_job_start <env>` prints `RUN_ARGV: host <h>, job <id>, env <env> (the pipeline's job protocol, D-113)`; on a host of the avoid list it holds the slot (`AVOID_HOLD_S`, 600 s) and exits 94 (D-102); otherwise it activates the environment as D-111 (a) does -- conda, else the environment's own folder -- and when neither starts holds the slot and exits 96; `pipeline_stage_job_end`, the job's EXIT trap, runs the chain's watchdog (D-111 (e)) whatever the job's exit. Launched by hand (no `PIPELINE_JOBS_DIR`), every launcher and job script behaves as before. (b) S2: a test task or a gate that could not start its command (94; an environment fault: 96, 97 / 98, a PBS start failure, the log's `RUN_ARGV ENV FAULT`) is submitted again, free, the fault counted on its host (D-102's count; D-111's guard, 12 in a row on 3 hosts, stops the stage); a gate that a failed `afterok` dependency ended unrun never ran and runs alone, at most 3 times in a row; when the gate refuses, the tasks that started and failed are rerun, at most 2 rounds (`S2_RERUN_ROUNDS`), a round whose bad tasks all never started (`no mea_env.json`) is free, and 3 free rounds in a row with no fewer unstarted tasks stop the stage (`FREE_ROUND_STALL`); a refusal no rerun fixes (a precondition, the environment) stops it as before. (c) S5: the test task, a gate (the round's or the gate alone) and the verification job that could not start are submitted again without counting a try; a round whose bad tasks all never started (no marker, no file, and a log that is absent, older than the round, without the job script's host line, or with `RUN_ARGV ENV FAULT` / `RUN_ARGV AVOID`) is free -- nothing quarantined -- under the same stall guard; any other round counts against `bank.max_rerun_rounds` as before (D-094). (d) S1: its jobs start through the protocol (the folder fallback included), but a well whose job could not start still stops S1 at the aggregation's refusal -- a residual (SBI SPEC sec. 8), S1 being skipped for LindDUP. (e) The constants are not configuration keys (`PIPELINE_S2_RERUN_ROUNDS`, `PIPELINE_FREE_ROUND_STALL` override them), so no configuration's digest changes.
- **Why:** the user's: D-111 is a property of the pipeline once started, whatever the data set. Under D-111 alone a node without conda would still have stopped LindDUP's S2 and S5 at a test task or a gate and ended the chain on purpose (v1.93, Open calls).
- **Implies:** the Sbi-extractor half reaches davinci only through the laptop (D-062): the run sheet's L0, then L2's pull and suites. SBI and Sbi-extractor go together: a launcher given `PIPELINE_JOBS_DIR` refuses a folder without `stage_job.sh` (`sbix_pipeline_v`), and the inputs check fails `protocol (Sbi-extractor)` on a checkout before D-113. ANN is unchanged: `run_mea_task.pbs` activates the environment and hands `submit_mea_array.sh` its prefix (`ENV_PREFIX`). The running Giulia chain (sequencer `1917793`) reads the pulled files through its successor and the jobs it submits; nothing it runs changed (blocks 2, 3 and 9 and new helpers; `giulia.json` untouched) `[CODE SBI@fa51751, the diff]`.
- **Does not decide:** D-111's residuals (a sequencer job PBS cannot start while nothing of the pipeline runs; a cluster where nothing starts) and S1's (above); whether the two deliberate ends become pauses (Open calls, D-111); the guard's constants.
- **Sources:** `[user]` 2026-10-29 17:37; v1.93's Open call; SBI `specs/SPEC.md` at `fa51751`; Sbi-extractor `specs/SPEC.md` at `4ef7576`; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-29]` the tests in Status.
- **Status:** active; BUILT 2026-10-29, not run on davinci. SBI `sci/pipeline` `fa51751` (on `c42631a`) `[GITHUB 2026-10-29, ls-remote]`; Sbi-extractor commit `4ef7576` (tree `9d8ea296`, on `8e95f86`), delivered for the laptop push (D-062), its GitHub branch still at `e9aeb55` `[GITHUB 2026-10-29, ls-remote]`. Tests `[SANDBOX 2026-10-29]`: SBI block 3 `test_env_rounds` (a node without conda, faults at the test task and the gate, PBS failing one start: PIPELINE DONE in 7 jobs, node faults `{broken: 2}`; a task failing twice: the gate a failed dependency left unrun run alone, rounds 1 and 2, then STOP; tasks that never start: free rounds 1-3, then the stall STOP); block 2 `test_env_faults` (VERIFIED in 8 jobs, one free round, no quarantine) and `test_env_decisions` (9 cases); block 7c `stage_job_units` (conda; the folder; 96 after the hold; 94; the command's own exit; the watchdog); the inputs check's protocol rows; mutations, each caught: 12 of the new stage and check code, `stage_avoid` and `stage_fault` of `stage_job.sh`, 5 in the inputs check's suite. ### D-115 -- Giulia's core cap is lowered to 300: 6 trials or members of 48 at once
- **Date / by:** 2026-10-30 / `[user]` (the Giulia build chat, 08:27, with the paste confirming D-111's pieces on davinci: "Can we please reduce the cores used to 300?").

- **Statement (Claude's reading):** the Giulia pipeline's jobs, the sequencer's 4 cores included, never ask for more than 300 cores at once: 296 for the stages, so 6 trials (or members, or re-detection tasks) of 48 at once (floor(296 / 48) = 6; 6 x 48 + 4 = 292). Read as Giulia's cap, not the two pipelines' sum: two minutes earlier the user told the LindDUP chat "I want to keep 200 cores for this pipeline" (v1.96), so the two together hold at most 500 `[reasoning]`. Nothing submitted is deleted (D-099): the jobs in flight when it loads finish, and a new one starts only when it fits -- with the eight round-5 trials running at 08:27, the pipeline is under 300 once two of them have ended, and from then on runs at most 6. A value under config v10: `CONFIG_VERSION` stays 10 (no key changed; `linddup.json` is v10 too); the digest changes, so the running chain loads it by `--stop` and a submission with `--accept-config-change` -- and any pull of the change must be followed by that reload at once, or the next sequencer job (the daily hand-over's successor, about 17:00 on 30 Oct) refuses the new digest and ends the chain on purpose (D-111's `CHAIN_END`).
- **Why:** the user's.
- **Implies:** by computation `[SANDBOX 2026-10-30, cap300_projection.py; its smoke test, 6 known-answer checks, passes; 20,000 simulated searches on rounds_by_400h.py's model from round 5's start, 06:02 CET on 30 Oct, the eight in flight then, the rest under the slot count; no queue waits or outages]`: a GP round of 10 lasts about 17% longer at 6 at once than at 8 -- 25.4 h against 21.7 h at the first run's trial lengths, 19.8 h against 16.9 h at rounds 1-3's pace -- so the 400 h clock (D-110) ends the search about one GP round earlier: after GP round 10 (5-95%: 10-11) instead of 11 (10-12), or after round 12 (11-13) instead of 13 (12-14), still on the evening of 5 Nov (the deadline for what S8 takes stays 09:18 CET on 5 Nov). The best fit's 10 members run 6 at once instead of 8.
- **Does not decide:** stopping running trials to be under 300 at once (not done, D-099; the user's call if the allocation needs it now -- each stopped trial would resume from its checkpoint and use one of its resubmissions); LindDUP's cap (D-109); the clock (D-110).
- **Sources:** `[user]` 2026-10-30 08:27; the LindDUP chat's v1.96 (08:25); D-099, D-101; `[SANDBOX 2026-10-30]` the projection; `[CODE SBI@356d228]` `giulia.json`, `tests/test_smoke_block1c.py`.

### D-116 -- The Giulia pipeline is halted and its jobs deleted: the simulated data it uses are corrupted
- **Date / by:** 2026-11-02 / `[user]` (the Giulia build chat, 09:54, with a `qstat` of seven jobs on `cpu`: the sequencer `1939402` R 01:32 and its successor `1941090` H, both `pl_giulia`; five trials R -- `t_58ac450` (`1940618`) 04:31, `t_70fca2d` (`1940837`) 01:56, `t_85ecdd9` (`1941193`) 00:30, `t_9e87bbf` (`1941875`) 00:11, `t_c5aff67` (`1941920`) 00:10).
- **Binds:** the Giulia pipeline's run on davinci (work root `/davinci-1/home/ldellamea/pipeline/giulia`, chain `pl_giulia`): its sequencer chain and S7's trial jobs; S8, the best fit, does not start. Not LindDUP (its own chain `pl_linddup`, work root and data).
- **Decision (the user's words):** "Can we please delete all the jobs here because the simulated data used are corrupted"
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** every job of the Giulia pipeline leaves the queue and none is submitted again until a later decision. (1) The sequencer is stopped first, `run_pipeline.sh --project giulia --stop --wait 180`: the held successor is deleted at once, and the running sequencer stops at its next tick, deletes its successor and writes `driver/CHAIN_END` (a deliberate end, D-111), so no stage job's watchdog revives the chain. (2) Then the trial jobs, selected by `CHAIN_NAME=pl_giulia` in their `Variable_List` (every pipeline job carries it, `stages/common.py` `qsub_line`), are deleted with `qdel` -- refused while any `pl_giulia` job is still queued, held or running. Not a plain `qdel` of the seven: a sequencer ended by a signal is an unclean end that hands over to its successor and writes no `CHAIN_END` (`pipeline.py:1396-1400`), so a stage job ending afterwards finds the chain gone without a deliberate end and submits a sequencer again (`jobs/chain.sh` `pipeline_watchdog`), which resubmits the deleted trials with `--resume` -- the opposite of the request `[CODE SBI@356d228; block 7c's watchdog case: with the stop first nothing is revived, without it the chain is]`. Nothing on disk is deleted -- the state, the log, the ledger, the checkpoints, the runs and the banks stay as they are, for the diagnosis (the standing rule: Claude never deletes data; the user does, through a list-then-delete tool with a CODE). "The simulated data used" is read as the data the search trains and scores on -- the simulated bank (S5), and S2's re-detection it was built from `[reasoning]`; which of them is corrupted, and how it showed, the user has not yet said.
- **Why:** the user's: the search's trials run on corrupted simulated data.
- **Implies:** `[reasoning]` if the corruption is in the simulated bank -- the only simulated data the search reads -- every trial of the restarted search since round 0 (19 Oct) was trained, selected and scored on it, so the ledger's nll values, the GP's ranking and the finalist they would name are suspect, not only the five trials deleted now. A submission on this work root (`run_pipeline.sh --project giulia`) removes `CHAIN_END` and resumes the same search on the same data, the deleted trials resubmitted with `--resume`: no submission until the call that follows. The clock: 328.6 h of the 400 (D-110) had elapsed at 09:54 CET; no round is proposed while no sequencer runs.
- **Does not decide:** which data are corrupted and what is rebuilt (S2's re-detection, the bank S5, or both; a rebuilt bank needs its record and certificate set aside first -- the user's, through a list-then-confirm tool); whether the search restarts from nothing (rounds set aside by `tools/search_reset.py`, D-100's way) or keeps any part; the clock of a new search (D-110's 400 h or another); the edge-support call (Open calls), on hold while S8 cannot start; LindDUP, whose jobs the lines do not select.
- **Sources:** `[user]` 2026-11-02 09:54 and its `qstat`; `[CODE SBI@356d228]` `pipeline.py` `cmd_stop` (1459-1522) and the sequencer's end (1396-1411), `jobs/chain.sh` `pipeline_watchdog` (134-164), `jobs/run_argv.pbs` (138-146), `stages/common.py` `qsub_line` (163-166); `[SANDBOX 2026-11-02]` the lines on a mock PBS, blocks 1b and 7c at `356d228`.
- **Status:** active; the lines H1-H4 sent 2026-11-02 (H1 the read-only list, H2 the stop, H3 the deletion, H4 the check) `[SANDBOX 2026-11-02]`: on a mock PBS with PBS Pro's wrapped `qstat -f`, H1 lists exactly the seven, H3 refuses while a `pl_giulia` job is queued, held, waiting or running and otherwise deletes exactly the five trials -- LindDUP's two jobs, an unrelated job, an array parent `1544754[]` and a look-alike chain `pl_giulia_old` untouched -- and H4 prints `Giulia jobs left: 0`; the chain-name filter right at all 90 split positions of the wrap for `pl_giulia` and three look-alikes; qselect's ids one per line or on one line; block 1b (the stop) 6 pass, 1 skip; block 7c 6 pass, 1 skip (test_edge_cases: the watchdog revives a chain gone without a deliberate end, and with a stop first revives nothing; test_invariants: the deliberate ends). Not yet run on davinci. [2026-11-02 10:22: superseded in part by D-117 -- the search's folders and the simulated bank are deleted, by the user with the purge tool's CODE; the halt and the no submission until the call that follows stand.]

### D-117 -- The Giulia search's folders and the simulated bank are deleted
- **Date / by:** 2026-11-02 / `[user]` (the Giulia build chat, 10:22, "remove everything on the this about this remove please"; asked what to remove, the card's answer "Search + simulated bank" -- "On davinci: the search's results and the simulated bank giulia_sim it trained on").

- **Decision (the user's words):** "remove everything on the this about this remove please"; the card: "Search + simulated bank".
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** deleted by the user through a list-then-delete tool with a CODE (the standing rule): `/davinci-1/home/ldellamea/tune_joint_giulia/S-A1` (the ledger, the pending specs, the runs and their checkpoints, the trial jobs' logs and argv files, the gate's probe files, `bank_split.json`, the rounds file); every `S-A1_set_aside_*` beside it (round 0 of 18-19 Oct, set aside by D-100 -- trained on the same bank); `/davinci-1/home/ldellamea/runs/giulia/giulia_best` if it exists (S8 never started); `/davinci-1/home/ldellamea/banks/giulia_sim` (the shards, sidecars, task markers, the record -- sha256 `17f13161a87c1dc4` when it was certified `[CLUSTER 2026-10-18]` -- and the certificate) and `.../banks/giulia_sim_quarantine` (the bad shards of 18 Oct, D-095). Kept: the real bank `giulia_real` (S6), the bank plan's folder (S4, with the bank launcher's logs), the pipeline's work root (the state, the log, the verification reports, the bank profile), S1-S3's artefacts, S2's re-detected simulations, the diagnostic files in the home. The tool lists, prints a CODE, and deletes only with that CODE; it refuses while the work root's lock exists or any job of the chain `pl_giulia` is queued, held or running, so the deletion follows the halt (D-116's lines H1-H4). The read of the card: "the search's results" is the search's folders whole -- the set-aside round 0 and the best fit's folder included -- and "the simulated bank" includes its quarantine `[reasoning]`; both are named on the listing before the CODE is typed.
- **Why:** the user's: the simulated data the search used are corrupted (D-116).
- **Implies:** a new search needs a new simulated bank first: S5 rebuilt, then S7 from nothing (no round left to set aside), then S8. S5 builds the bank from S2's re-detected simulations, so if the corruption is upstream -- in S2's outputs or the campaign -- a rebuilt bank inherits it `[reasoning]`. The state file still records S5 done and S7 running: a later run needs `--retry sim_bank --retry search` (the dry run shows it), and the budget gate's probe jobs run again (their files go with the campaign). Once deleted, what remains of the bank is the verification reports in the work root and the purge record `<work_root>/purge/PURGE_<utc>.json` (paths, file counts, bytes, the record's sha256 prefix).
- **Does not decide:** what was corrupted and how it showed (not yet said); whether S2's re-detected simulations and the bank plan are sound for a rebuild; when S5-S8 run again and on which clock (D-110); the edge-support call; the home's diagnostic files (kept).

### D-118 -- A synthetic experiment tests arms A2 and A5 through the pipeline: a synthetic "real" cohort and simulations over the whole parameter box
- **Date / by:** 2026-11-03 / `[user]` (the LindDUP chat, 09:13, while the simulations are redone).
- **Binds:** a new synthetic test of the joint stack and of the pipeline, a subproject of its own (its plan and roadmap to settle, roadmap rule 7): the bench's bank builder and generators (`hpc/joint/stage1/`, `hpc/dsn/latent_burst_generator.py`, `generate_burst_data.py`), the runner's arms A2 and A5 (`hpc/joint/stage3/run_joint_arms.py`), the tuner's campaigns S-A2 and S-A5 (`hpc/joint/stage4/`), the pipeline (`hpc/pipeline/`, a project folder of its own). Not LindDUP's or Giulia's runs, data or configurations.
- **Decision (the user's words, spelling corrected):** "While waiting for the simulations to be redone I would like you to set up a synthetic experiment to test the whole pipeline with the synthetic approach already used for the DSN. I would like now to test the arm 2 and the arm 5, I think, where we use the real data. You have therefore to generate the synthetic 'real' data and also simulations across the whole parameter space."
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** arms A2 (NPE + lambda_dsn times the DSN metric loss on the real windows' class labels) and A5 (NPE + lambda_rep times the replicate-consistency term on same-donor pairs of real wells) -- the two joint arms whose extra term reads the real data (the arms table, deck pack B.5; `JOINT_DSN_NPE_USAGE_v1.md` sec. 5.1) -- are tested end to end on synthetic data: (1) a simulated bank whose theta covers the whole parameter box, uniformly, as a simulation campaign's prior does (D-078), not the bench's class-mixture prior (plan eq. 7); (2) a synthetic "real" cohort with phenotype classes, donors and wells, whose theta is drawn by class, recorded and withheld from training, so every posterior can be scored against the truth (the pseudo-real endpoint, plan S4.0). Both come from the synthetic approach of the DSN's own experiments: `generate_burst_data.py`'s burst model (its control and pathological parameter sets) and `latent_burst_generator.py`'s latent box, which the bench builder reuses unchanged (`--provider dsn`; `--provider bench` is a separate generator of the same burst family). "I think" read as tentative on the arm numbers; the arms table confirms that A2 and A5 are the joint arms trained on real data `[KB]`.
- **Why:** the user's: the pipeline waits for the redone simulations, and A2 and A5 have never run, on the bench or through the pipeline.
- **Implies:** code, to specify first (SPEC): a box prior for the simulated arm, and a sidecar the runner reads as the unit box (L0 = 0); the cohort's design in the builder; the pipeline's side (a synthetic data set has no recordings and no MEA campaign, so S1-S4 do not apply). The A5 test meets three open findings of the joint documentation set `[CODE SBI@68cc8a7]`: F-ab (`enumerate_donor_pairs` pairs rows by donor only, so two windows of one well count as a replicate pair), F-at (`donor`, `well`, `batch` restart at 0 in every shard and `concat_shards` does not offset them), F-aa (A5 with no same-donor pair trains as A1 silently).
- **Does not decide:** the generator, the cohort's classes, donors and wells, the phenotype's shape, the window shape, the gap (pi), the budget, the cores, the evaluation endpoints -- asked 2026-11-03.
- **Sources:** `[user]` 2026-11-03 09:13; `[KB]` deck pack B.5, `JOINT_DSN_NPE_USAGE_v1.md` secs. 3.2-3.3 and 5.1, the joint documentation set's index (F-aa, F-ab, F-at); `[CODE SBI@68cc8a7]` `hpc/joint/stage1/build_latent_bank.py`, `latent_sbi_simulator.py`, `stage2/joint_batches.py:239-260`, `stage3/run_joint_arms.py:333-346, 572`, `stage4/npe_tune_joint.py:123, 186`; `hpc/dsn/latent_burst_generator.py:163-200`, `generate_burst_data.py:139-151`.

### D-119 -- The synthetic A2/A5 experiment's design: the bench generator; three conditions of medium difficulty on donors of two wells; the gap at pi = 0 and pi = 0.3; the pipeline's searches at a small budget
- **Date / by:** 2026-11-03 / `[user]` (the LindDUP chat; the card of four questions on D-118's design, answered 09:27).
- **Binds:** D-118's experiment: the banks it builds (`hpc/joint/stage1/build_latent_bank.py`), the arms A2 and A5 on its cohort (`hpc/joint/stage3/run_joint_arms.py`), the searches S-A2 and S-A5 (`hpc/joint/stage4/`), the pipeline's synthetic project (`hpc/pipeline/`). Not LindDUP's or Giulia's runs, data or configurations.
- **Decision (the user's words, spelling corrected, with the option each answer chose):** (1) the generator: "Bench generator" (the option: "The joint stack's 10-axis burst generator (7 class-bearing axes), scaled per unit like the real observable"; the card recommended the DSN latent generator); (2) the cohort: "the first option but with three conditions (make the difficulty of discerning the conditions medium" (the first option: "Donors with 2 wells: e.g. 9 donors per class x 2 wells = 36 wells (DUP15HD has 35); A5 pairs the two wells of each donor, as the joint plan intends"); (3) the gap: "No gap + one gap level" (the option: "Gap severity pi = 0 and pi = 0.3 (shifted parameter ranges, slow drift, contaminated windows)"; the card recommended no gap first); (4) the runs: "Pipeline, small budget (Recommended)" (the option: "The one-command pipeline: campaigns S-A2 and S-A5 (each also covers A1), e.g. 20 initial + 4 GP rounds of 10 = 60 trials each, then the best fit").
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** (1) both banks come from `--provider bench`: theta in the unit box (0, 1)^10, the class-bearing axes 0-6 (`burst_rate`, `irregularity`, `ibi_cv`, `n_fragments`, `intraburst_rate`, `participation_mean`, `burst_duration`) and the free axes 7-9 (`fragment_duty`, `participation_kappa`, `background`), the per-unit-mean observable `[CODE SBI@68cc8a7 bench_burst_provider.py:82-97]`; arm S draws theta uniformly on the box (D-118), arm R from the conditions' prior. (2) Arm R has C = 3 conditions; every donor has two wells sharing its theta, one realisation per well, and A5's pairs are the two wells of one donor. "Medium" carries no number yet, and the option's "9 donors per class" was an example written for two classes, so the cohort's size is open as well. (3) Two arm-R banks, pi = 0 and pi = 0.3, with the three gap modes `range_shift`, `drift` and `contamination`; arm S is at pi = 0 by construction (the builder refuses any other value on arm S). (4) Each arm-R bank is searched with S-A2 and with S-A5 -- four searches; S-A2 nests S-A1 at `dsn_on` = 0 and S-A5 at `rep_on` = 0 (`joint_space.py:457-470`), which is the card's "each also covers A1" -- of about 60 trials each (20 initial and 4 rounds of 10), then each search's best fit.
- **Why:** the user's.
- **Implies:** besides D-118's F-ab, F-at, F-aa and the box prior, the design meets four findings of the joint documentation set in the code `[CODE SBI@68cc8a7]`: F-av -- the bench provider's range shift raises as soon as one donor's `fragment_duty` coordinate exceeds 1 - 0.35 pi, so a bank of 27 donors at pi = 0.3 fails with probability 1 - 0.895^27 = 0.950 `[SANDBOX]`, and where it builds, the shift is for most donors an exact offset of the free coordinates (F-bj): the pi = 0.3 bank needs a decision; F-ba -- the bench's class centres (`simplex_centres`) are not a regular simplex at C = 3 (pairwise distances 1.065, 0.805, 0.697 in theta; the DSN generator's regular ones 0.737 each at its spacing `[SANDBOX]`), so one pair of conditions is harder than the other two at every tau_ov; F-t -- the DSN loss's class count is read from the simulated bank (`run_joint_arms.py:672`, `len(np.unique(sim["cls"]))`), which a box-prior arm S does not label, so A2 on the cohort needs the count from the metric source; F-aw -- the two arms need distinct seeds.
- **Does not decide:** the number for "medium" and the class geometry; the donors per condition and the windows per well; the range shift at pi = 0.3; the window shape, the size of arm S, the seeds, the cores; the endpoints; the roadmap's plan (roadmap rule 7) -- put to the user 2026-11-03.
- **Sources:** `[user]` the card, 2026-11-03 09:27; `[KB]` the joint documentation set's index (F-av, F-ba, F-bj, F-t, F-aw; E2, E3, E6 rows of its sec. 8); `[CODE SBI@68cc8a7]` `hpc/joint/stage1/build_latent_bank.py:90-125, 160-260`, `latent_sbi_simulator.py:128-151, 165-240`, `bench_burst_provider.py:82-131, 175-218`, `stage3/run_joint_arms.py:75-111, 341-346, 672`, `stage4/joint_space.py:456-477`; `hpc/dsn/latent_burst_generator.py:460-527`; `[SANDBOX]` the calibration of the Status line (`calib_medium.py`, standalone).
- **Status:** active; the open points put to the user 2026-11-03. [2026-11-03 10:05: "medium" computed on the bench generator in the sandbox `[SANDBOX]` -- a cohort built as arm R is (theta per donor, two wells, one realisation per well, the default nuisance nested batch:donor:well, pi = 0) with balanced conditions, 200-s windows at 100 Hz; three levels of separability: the theta ceiling (the Bayes accuracy of the condition from the donor's theta, which no classifier of that donor's data can exceed), one window and one donor (a fixed linear classifier on the runner's eight window statistics, A_ref's summary, cross-validated by donor; chance 0.333). With the DSN generator's regular simplex (its `_class_center_vectors`) at 0.6 of its spacing and tau_ov 0.10, four independent cohorts (24, 50, 40 and 40 donors per condition; 3, 6, 6 and 6 windows per well) read one window at 0.648-0.674 (mean 0.657), one donor at 0.650-0.700 (mean 0.686), the theta ceiling at 0.973-0.976, and the three pairs of conditions at 0.735-0.846; one cohort each at 0.5 of the spacing reads 0.581 / 0.620 / 0.941 (window / donor / ceiling), at 0.75 0.739 / 0.773 / 0.994, at the full spacing 0.852 / 0.875 / 1.000. The bench's own centres read 0.715 / 0.764 / 1.000 at tau_ov 0.10, with pairs 0.927 / 0.969 / 0.701, and 0.583-0.677 / 0.556-0.700 / 0.947-0.948 at 0.20 (two cohorts), with pairs 0.801-0.816 / 0.875-0.903 / 0.597-0.663 (F-ba). Pooling a donor's windows barely raises the accuracy: the confusions are whole donors that read as another condition, not noisy windows [reasoning, from these numbers]. Proposed: medium = one-window accuracy near 2/3, halfway between chance and perfect.] [2026-11-03 11:21: the open points settled on the user's second card: D-120.]

### D-120 -- The synthetic A2/A5 experiment's open points: "medium" is the regular simplex at 0.6 of the DSN spacing; 9 donors per condition; the duty clipped at 1 at pi = 0.3; the roadmap `synth`, with 180-s windows, a 120-core cap and distinct seeds
- **Date / by:** 2026-11-03 / `[user]` (the LindDUP chat; the second card, on D-119's open points, answered 11:21).
- **Binds:** D-118's and D-119's experiment: arm R's class geometry and size, the pi = 0.3 bank, arm S's size, the window shape, the project `synth` of the pipeline (its tag, its core cap, its seeds), the roadmap `synth` (SBI `sci/pipeline`, `specs/roadmap/synth.yaml`). Not LindDUP's or Giulia's runs, data or configurations.
- **Decision (the user's words and choices):** (1) "Regular x0.6 (Recommended)" (the option: "Equidistant centres at 0.6 of the DSN spacing, tau 0.10: 1 window 0.66, 1 donor 0.69, theta ceiling 0.97; pairs 0.74-0.85"); (2) "9 per condition (Recommended)" (the option: "27 donors, 54 wells, 6 windows of 200 s per well (1200 s, like a DUP15HD recording): 324 windows"); (3) "Clip duty at 1 (Recommended)" (the option: "Keep your three gap modes; a donor whose shifted duty would pass 1 gets duty 1, i.e. a smaller shift for that donor"); (4) on the roadmap and its defaults, the user's words, spelling corrected: "1) 200-core cap, distinct seeds for arms S and R? No, 120 are ok, and yes two different seeds for S and R. 2) 180-s windows at 100 Hz. For the rest is ok the recommended option" (the recommended option: "Yes, with theory lines": theory groups on S1 -- separability; the gap as misspecification --, S3 -- the metric and replicate terms; F-bm -- and S6 -- the endpoints; E7).
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** (1) arm R's condition centres are mu_c = 0.5 + 0.6 (m_c - 0.5) for c in {0, 1, 2}, where m_c in [0, 1]^7 are the DSN generator's regular simplex centres for C = 3 on the bench's seven class-bearing axes (`latent_burst_generator._class_center_vectors(3, 7, "simplex")`: pairwise distance 0.7366 and centred cosines -0.5 `[SANDBOX]`), so the centres are 0.442 apart; tau_ov = 0.10; the setting was chosen for its separability (one window near 2/3; D-119's Status), and it is the setting that is decided, not a target accuracy. (2) 9 donors per condition, balanced (27 donors), two wells per donor sharing its theta, six windows per well -- at the 180-s windows of (4), 1080 s per well, DUP15HD's six windows per 1200-s subregion trace `[KB]` (E1) -- so 54 wells and 324 windows. (3) The pi = 0.3 bank keeps the three gap modes; the range shift displaces each free axis's range by pi * 0.35 = 0.105 of its width, and the shifted `fragment_duty` is clipped at 1, so a donor whose shifted duty L' (1 - phi_duty) + U' phi_duty, with L' = 0.41825 and U' = 1.06825, exceeds 1 -- that is, phi_duty > 0.895 -- gets duty 1 and a smaller shift. (4) The roadmap `synth` on SBI `sci/pipeline` with the six steps of the reply (S1 design and SPEC; S2 synthetic banks; S3 arms A2 and A5 on a cohort; S4 the pipeline's synthetic data set; S5 runs on davinci; S6 evaluation) and theory groups on S1, S3 and S6 with the three lines; windows of 180 s at 100 Hz, W = 18,000 (the user's correction of the proposed 200 s); arm S of 20,000 theta drawn uniformly on the box, two wells each, one window per well (40,000 windows); the project tag `synth`; the core cap 120 (D-099's cap, set per project) in place of the proposed 200; distinct seeds for arms S and R (the operating rule of F-aw).
- **Why:** the user's.
- **Implies:** S1 writes the formulation into `specs/SPEC.md`; S2 implements in the builder: the box prior and its sidecar kind, the regular centres with a scale, balanced conditions, the distinct seeds, bank-wide ids (F-at), the clipped duty (F-av); S3: F-t, F-ab, F-aa; S4: the pipeline's synthetic kind and `projects/synth/synth.json` (tag `synth`, `resources.max_cores` 120). A cohort of 27 donors is one draw of a law whose separability varies from draw to draw (Status), so the built arm-R banks get their separability measured and recorded.
- **Does not decide:** the endpoints in detail (S6); the shards, walltimes and concurrency; the search's settings beyond D-119's budget (the epochs, the steps per epoch and the rest: Giulia's values or others, to propose at S4).
- **Sources:** `[user]` the second card, 2026-11-03 11:21; `[KB]` `claude/joint_docs/00_INDEX.md` (E1's row of sec. 8: six windows per 1200-s subregion trace); `[CODE SBI@68cc8a7]` `hpc/dsn/latent_burst_generator.py:460-527`, `hpc/joint/stage1/bench_burst_provider.py:82-131`, `latent_gap.py:97-109`; `[SANDBOX]` `calib_medium.py` (standalone), the runs of the Status line.

### D-121 -- The synthetic searches rank on the simulated report NLL, as deployed, and the evaluation reads the pseudo-real NLL; R0 and R3 are independent cohorts
- **Date / by:** 2026-11-03 / `[user]` (the LindDUP chat; the third card, on the two open questions of `specs/SPEC.md` sec. 8, answered 11:58).
- **Binds:** D-118..D-120's experiment: the objective of its four searches (the pipeline's search of `hpc/pipeline/stages/search.py` and `hpc/joint/stage4/npe_tune_joint.py`, unchanged), the evaluation of the roadmap's step S6, the seeds of its arm-R banks (the builder's `--seed`). Not LindDUP's or Giulia's runs.
- **Decision (the user's words, spelling corrected):** on the objective -- the card's options were "Simulated NLL, as deployed (Recommended)", "Pseudo-real NLL, held-out donors" and "Both" -- "I want to be sure: A2 is the arm that works only on simulated data while A5 on simulated data plus the real ones acting on the DSN alone, right? For the rest as you propose"; on the cohorts: "Two seeds, independent" (the card recommended one seed, paired).
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** (1) the four searches rank their trials on the ledger's `nll`, the simulated report split's NLL L (`npe_tune_joint.py:623`), as the pipeline does on real data, whose theta is unknown -- the recommended option, which "for the rest as you propose" accepts; L_pseudo_real, the NLL of arm R's withheld theta, ranks nothing and is read by the evaluation, which compares within each search the best trial with the extra term on (dsn_on = 1 in S-A2, rep_on = 1 in S-A5) and the best A1 trial, each picked on L, on L_pseudo_real paired by donor; no tuner change. (2) R0 and R3 are built on two seeds s_R0 and s_R3, distinct from each other and from arm S's s_S: two independent cohorts of 27 donors, so the comparison of pi = 0 with pi = 0.3 carries the cohort-to-cohort spread of the separability as well as the gap. (3) The user's question is answered in the reply of the same turn: the premise is half right -- A2 too uses the real windows, through their condition labels; the arm on simulated data alone is A1; both A2's and A5's real-data terms reach the encoder alone (the DSN metric loss is a function of the embeddings only; the replicate term runs under a stop-gradient on the flow's weights) `[CODE SBI@dd4ab88 run_joint_arms.py:178-205, stage2/joint_train.py:291-323; KB deck pack B.5, sources ledger B6]`. Whether the corrected picture changes the choice of (1) is the user's to say.
- **Why:** the user's; (1) is the deployable selection, the one the pipeline can make on real data.

- **Decision (the user's words):** "rise the memory" -- option A of Claude's reply of the same morning, "raise the memory. Set the trial and best-fit memory to 1.5 x the worst of the 15 peaks, rounded up to a multiple of 8 GB" (the reply's "x" a multiplication sign), with the read-only table of the 15 probes pasted, ending `15 probe file(s) of 15; worst peak 59.3 GB; x1.5 = 88.9 GB -> 96gb`.
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** both memory requests 96gb; the space (D-123's), the 8 cores, the trials' 24 h and the members' 120 h unchanged. The restart retries the failed search with the configuration change accepted (`--retry search_S-A2-R0 --accept-config-change`); a search folder's identity does not include the memory (`hpc/pipeline/stages/search.py:141-149`), so the 15 probe files are reused and each gate decides at the sequencer's first ticks, with no new probe job.

The table `[CLUSTER 2026-11-04 11:42, the user's paste]`: in all five searches the costliest configuration peaks at 19.0, 32.4 and 59.2-59.3 GB at batch 256, 512 and 1024 -- the same in A1, A2 and A5, and linear in the batch, about 5.6 GiB plus 54 MiB per window (a least-squares line through the 15 points, largest residual 0.08 GiB `[SANDBOX 2026-11-04, numpy.polyfit]`); 15.0-17.5, 28.3-34.0 and 53.6-67.7 s per step at 40, 20 and 10 steps per epoch; every trial projected within 3.4-4.5 h, with the safety factor within 5.1-6.7 h against 24 h. So memory was the only bound, and the alternative offered (32gb without batch 1024) would have failed too: batch 512 peaks at 32.4 GB. Claude's reasons for A: it keeps the agreed space; up to about 125 GiB an 8-core job still packs six to a 48-core node of about 754 GiB (`dvnode031`, `resources_available.mem` 790,779,544 kb, 2026-10-07 `[CLUSTER]`); 1.5 is the gate's own safety factor on time; Giulia's gate measured a peak of the same order, 56.27 GB, under her 160gb (D-081's Status).

### D-126 -- The synthetic experiment's evaluation (step S6): an ensemble per arm, the literature's figures per arm and per comparison, four data sets, a standalone tool with davinci jobs
- **Date / by:** 2026-11-11 / `[user]` (the LindDUP chat: the message of 12:35 and the answers on Claude's card the same turn).
- **Binds:** the roadmap `synth`'s step S6, the evaluation of the run of D-125: which models are compared, on which data, with which figures, and where the code runs; `specs/SPEC.md` sec. 2.5 and blocks 18-22. Not the searches' ranking (D-121) and not Giulia's or LindDUP's runs.
- **Decision (the user's words):** "Also write a code to plot everyting is usually plotted in literature. so pictures for each arm and comparison picutres"; on the card -- the models: "5-seed ensembles, both arms (Recommended)", the option that trains the four missing best fits (20 member jobs) so that every search with a term has an ensemble of each arm; the figures: all four families offered, "Global calibration", "Accuracy & sharpness", "Posterior views & checks", "Embeddings & search"; the data: "Simulated test split", "R0 cohort (pi = 0)", "R3 cohort (pi = 0.3)", "R3 against theta* + delta too"; where: "Standalone tool + davinci job (Recommended)" (the answers as this chat recorded them; the card's wording of each option is Claude's).
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** (1) The models: in each search q of S-A2 or S-A5 (S-A2-R0, S-A5-R0, S-A2-R3, S-A5-R3), the best trial on L -- the simulated report split's NLL, the searches' own ranking (D-121) -- of the arm the search's finalist is not (A1, or the term's arm A2 or A5) is fitted as a second ensemble with the best fit's values (five members, split seed 1, member seeds 1-5, 40 passes in 40 epochs, patience 10, 8 cores, 96gb, 120 h): the stage `best_fit_alt_<q>` of `synth.json` v3 (`alt_best_fit`, block 18). So each such q has E_{q,A1} and E_{q,A(q)}, sharing the split and the member seeds. S-A1's best fit is the A1 ensemble of its own search, scored on R0 and R3 (D-122). (2) The data: arm S's report split under split seed 1 (theta known), R0 and R3 with the withheld theta*, and R3 against the pseudo-true parameter of the range shift theta^dag (theta* + delta on axes 8 and 9, min(theta*_7 + delta, 1) on `fragment_duty`, delta = 0.105; pseudo-true for the shift alone, the drift and the contamination having no coordinate in the box). (3) The figures, per arm (one ensemble of one search) and per comparison (the two arms of a search; R0 against R3): calibration -- the expected HPD coverage with each member's curve and the ensemble's, the marginal SBC ranks (ECDFs, histograms), TARP; accuracy and sharpness -- recovery (posterior mean and 90 % interval against theta), z-score against contraction, marginal SD against the prior's, the NLL per window and per donor, the donor-paired forest plot; posterior views and checks -- corner plots of example donors, the posterior-predictive check with the bench generator, L-C2ST; embeddings and the search -- the members' embeddings of arm S and of the cohort on two principal components by condition with their MMD, the GP traces, L against L_pseudo_real over all trials, the learning curves. (4) Where: a standalone tool reading the run's outputs, one davinci job per ensemble that samples and saves `.npz`, a separate step drawing the figures; not pipeline stages (blocks 19-22, `hpc/pipeline/evaluation/` proposed).
- **Why:** the user's. Claude's reason for (1), given on the card: the decision rule pairs two arms' models within a search (claude/deck_pack/08_SEC_G, G.0-G.2), and the run fitted only the finalist's arm, so without the other arm's ensemble the comparison would set a five-member ensemble at 40 passes against single-seed trials at 6 passes.
- **Implies:** SBI `sci/pipeline` `e92d451`: block 18 -- the tuner's `finalists --arm/--out` (`hpc/joint/stage4/npe_tune_joint.py`), the key `synthetic.searches[i].alt_best_fit` (`config.ALT_ARMS`: S-A2 and S-A5 only), the stage `RunAltBestFitStage` (`stages/synthetic.py`: S8 with its own folder `<runs_root>/<q>/synth_best_alt`, member jobs `ba_synth_s<k>`, and a finalist chosen from the tuner's two per-arm rankings, the search's finalist having to be the best of exactly one), `synth.json` v3 (true on the four searches), the README's run sheet "synth: the other arm's best fits" (X1-X5, every line with `--accept-config-change`); `specs/SPEC.md` sec. 2.5 (the evaluation in full notation: (E1) the ensemble's mixture, (E2) the expected coverage, (E3) the donor-paired difference), block 18 and blocks 19-22 drafted, their open choices Q-E1..Q-E6 in sec. 8; the roadmap's I18-I22 under S6 (`5bd7891`). ### D-127 -- The evaluation's six open choices as Claude proposed them (Q-E1..Q-E6)
- **Date / by:** 2026-11-11 / `[user]` (the LindDUP chat, the message of 14:38).
- **Binds:** the synthetic experiment's evaluation (D-126; the roadmap synth's step S6): the estimators, the rows and the settings of `specs/SPEC.md` sec. 2.5 and blocks 19-22, whose open choices were sec. 8's Q-E1..Q-E6. Not the searches' ranking (D-121) and not the ensembles' training (D-126, block 18).
- **Decision (the user's words):** "I confirm go on please" -- the reply to Claude's message that listed the six proposals (the brackets of `specs/SPEC.md` sec. 8 at `5bd7891`).
- **Statement (Claude's reading):** the six as proposed. Q-E1: 1,000 posterior draws per row on every data set, 200 from each of the five members (the HPD rank's Monte Carlo SD at most 0.016 per row). Q-E2: D_sim is the whole report split of split seed 1, about 1,000 donors of arm S with two windows each; the NLL over both windows, the calibration tests (HPD coverage, SBC, TARP) over one window per donor; on the cohorts every window, the curves' bands from a donor-cluster bootstrap. Q-E3: where theta-dagger leaves the open box or lies on its boundary (in expectation 0.28 of R3's donors), the NLL and the HPD coverage over the donors inside, with their count; the marginal ranks, the recovery and the z-scores over every donor. Q-E4: the posterior-predictive check -- for each cohort window 20 draws of the ensemble, each simulated by the bench generator with a fresh nuisance and no gap, compared with the window on the eight fixed statistics of arm A_ref's summary; the same on 324 rows of D_sim as the reference. Q-E5: L-C2ST -- sbi 0.27's `LC2ST`, calibrated on 10,000 fresh windows of arm S, one per theta, at a seed of its own, one ensemble draw per window; features theta and the eight statistics; sbi's default MLP; the null by 100 permutations; the local statistic at every cohort window and its donor mean. Q-E6: one CPU job on davinci after the sampling jobs; PNG at 200 dpi and PDF under `<runs_root>/evaluation/`; the corner plots' example donors one per condition, the donor of median lbar_d under the A1 ensemble, so the choice is not tuned to an arm.
- **Why:** the user's. Claude's reasons, given with the proposals: rows sharing theta make the uniformity tests anti-conservative (the deck pack's G.6 (i): an exact posterior fails G2 in 0.975 of replicates with 16 rows per theta, in 0.055 with one), hence one window per donor of arm S; the practical guide's case study ("Simulation Based Inference A Practical Guide", Fig. 10, `[KB, full text]`) calibrated L-C2ST on 20k additional simulations, and D_sim's 1,000 donors would be few; the PPC in the model's own world (no gap) reads the misfit the gap leaves.
- **Implies:** SBI `sci/pipeline` `0d753a0` (block 19, the sampler), `2d7411f` (block 22, part 1: the calibration bank and the samplings' launcher), `e92fb62` (block 20, the quantities), `4332dfd` (block 21, the figures), `61f4262` (block 22, part 2: the report), every block smoke-tested in the sandbox and documented in `specs/SPEC.md` blocks 19-22 (Q-E1..Q-E6 closed in sec. 8): `hpc/pipeline/evaluation/` -- `ev_sample.py` (one ensemble: the members rebuilt and checked against their own per-row NLL, the draws, the mixture's densities, the embeddings, the PPC windows, L-C2ST's calibration draws, per data set), `ev_metrics.py`, `ev_figures.py` (`FIGURES.md` lists each figure with a caption), `ev_report.py`, the launcher `submit_eval.sh --project synth` (`ev_submit.py`, standard library: the calibration bank, one sampling job per complete ensemble after it, the report after them; what is done or queued not submitted twice) and one job script `jobs/eval_run.pbs`; the values in `hpc/pipeline/projects/synth/synth_eval.json`, a file of its own so that the running chain's `synth.json` is untouched; the README's run sheet "synth: the evaluation" (E1-E9). Choices made at the build, Claude's and recorded in the SPEC, not the user's: the seeds common to the ensembles (common random numbers for the paired differences); the densities at the draws embed each window once (sbi 0.27's `ZukoFlow.log_prob` embeds it n_post times; the same numbers); L-C2ST's features z-scored and its classifiers seeded through `train_on_observed_data(seed=...)` (in sbi 0.27 the constructor's seed reaches the folds alone, and two runs differed); TARP's primary reference points read out of the windows' statistics (the amendment of sec. 2.5 at the drafting); the corner plots' donors from S-A1's A1 ensemble while a search's own A1 ensemble is not reported; the jobs' resources (calbank 1 core, 16gb, 4 h; sample 8, 64gb, 24 h; report 8, 64gb, 24 h); the report not submitted while nothing is sampled; the figures' colours after the dataviz method (arms and conditions as validated categorical triplets). Tests `[SANDBOX 2026-11-11]`: test_smoke_evaluation.py 14 of 15 checks pass, the convergence check skipped, from a fresh reduced project in 262 s; mutations caught: block 19 six, block 20 five (one after its test was strengthened), block 21 two, block 22 eight (four per part); test_smoke_pipeline and the synthetic T15, T16 and T18 checks still pass. Not run on davinci at this write: the samplings' commands were handed over at `59d7f90` (E1-E4).
- **Does not decide:** the evaluation's jobs' place in the core budget (Open calls); a correction for multiplicity across the four searches and two cohorts (open since D-126).

### D-131 -- A full audit is hierarchical: parallel tester runs per block group, then parallel runs over the full chains of operations, on Opus; the roadmap counts every report of a commit
- **Date / by:** 2026-11-13 / `[user]` (the tester-scheduling chat: the request of 09:17, "Please schedule agent runs over the new code implementations of the last month over the whole repository. Use opus as agent model", and the answer on a question card of about 09:45).
- **Binds:** every full audit by the scientific-coding tester, and an audit over a period of the work (the request of 09:17); the one-shot scheduled tasks "Scientific code tester: Simulation-Based-Inference ..." of 2026-11-13; `specs/roadmap/roadmap.py` on SBI `sci/pipeline` and the subproject-roadmap skill's copy; the scientific-coding skill's full audit (`references/night_run.md`).
- **Decision (the user's words, spelling corrected):** "three parallel runs (this parallel approach is perfect in general, do it hierarchical in the sense that if I told you to do the full audit you first do the single parallel runs and then a set of comprehensive parallel runs that covers how everything is concerted, that is the full chains of operations). Solve the problem with the roadmap as well" -- the card's "Three parallel runs" chosen over "One full audit"; the card's start time "Tonight 23:00"; the model from the request: Opus.
- **Statement (Claude's reading and design, `[proposed]` where it is not the user's):** (1) Tier 1: the SPEC's blocks are split into groups and each group is audited by its own tester run, all in parallel, in full mode restricted to the group -- its blocks, the status of earlier findings on them, their SPEC status lines and validation targets, and their boundaries with blocks outside the group, from the group's side. On SBI `sci/pipeline`, whose blocks 1-23 are all of the month's new code (2026-10-15..11-12): A = blocks 1-10 (sequencer, input and bank stages, runner, search and best fit, the stand-in end to end, the inputs check, the purge tool), B = blocks 11-17 (the synthetic A2/A5 experiment, the roadmap's steps S2-S4), C = blocks 18-23 (the evaluation, step S6). (2) Tier 2, after tier 1: one run per chain of operations, in parallel -- an end-to-end flow through the real entry points, across the groups -- testing the chain's oracles from the stage graph (SPEC secs. 2.1, 2.2, 2.4, 2.5), every producer-consumer boundary along it on the upstream stages' real outputs, resume and refusal along it, and the silent failures that only show across stages; block-level oracles are tier 1's, cited from its reports and not re-derived. On SBI: C1 the real-data project (`run_pipeline.sh --project giulia`, S1-S8, with the inputs check, the purge and the search tools around it); C2 the synthetic experiment from `synth.json` to the searches, the best fits and the other arm's best fits; C3 the evaluation from the best fits to the report and the data overview (`submit_eval.sh`). (3) Every run of one audit tests the same commit (the head of the branch when tier 1 starts), runs on Opus (`claude-opus-5-5`, any subagent on Opus too), has its own results branch and report (`<date>_<HHMM>_full_<run id>.md`), and numbers its findings in its own range (F101.., F201.., ...). (4) The roadmap: `roadmap.py` counts every report of a commit (a report on the branch and on its `tester/*` branch once), reads a full-mode report's block list when its Scope names blocks, takes a block's open findings from every report at the newest tested commit, and limits "testing" to the blocks named on a running run's status-file `Scope:` line.
- **Why:** the user's: parallel runs per group go deeper per block than one run over 23 blocks, and the chain runs test how the parts work together, which no group run sees whole. The roadmap: until this fix `roadmap.py` kept one report per commit, so two of three group reports would have left their blocks shown as untested.
- **Implies:** six runs for this audit (three at 23:00 Europe/Rome on 2026-11-13, three chain runs after them); the roadmap fix on `sci/pipeline` before they start; the scientific-coding skill's full audit rewritten to this rule (a change the user saves from a proposal card -- a chat cannot write the skill files), and the subproject-roadmap skill's `scripts/roadmap.py` and `scripts/test_roadmap.py` replaced by the user with the patched copies.
- **Does not decide:** the groups and chains of other repositories (drawn from their SPEC at their audit); whether an incremental night run whose scope spans several groups is split the same way (Claude's reading of "in general": yes, tier 1 only; not confirmed); the Sbi-extractor half of D-113 (`4ef7576`), still not on GitHub, which the runs can only record as missing.
- **Sources:** `[user]` 2026-11-13 09:17 and the card of about 09:45; `[GITHUB 2026-11-13]` SBI `sci/pipeline` 67 commits ahead of `main` (`9fcbc88`), 2026-10-15..11-12, 129 files, no other branch with a commit after 2026-10-07; the last tester report, `specs/test_reports/2026-10-20_1615_full.md` at `04cb7ab`, its triage column empty; Sbi-extractor `feat/real-arm-parity` at `e9aeb55` (ls-remote); `[CODE SBI@3c0fc73]` `specs/roadmap/roadmap.py`, `collect_reports` (one report per commit).
- **Status:** active; being set up in the tester-scheduling chat, 2026-11-13.
